# Cross-examination, Round 3: RPR-0.4-DRAFT and RPR-0.4_REVIEW_RESPONSE

RPR-0.4 stays rejected, and the response still isn't enough to write the next specification from. Codex's supplied claims hold, except one: equal-time price events are ordered deterministically, but the order can't be certified. I also found six errors or omissions in my own Round 2, and ten new source-backed findings (N7–N16).

The most important new finding is that Production's **execution decision** reads wallet-derived state. So "decision parity" and "no reads of Production wallet/position data" can't both hold unless the user decides which levels of parity Replay may claim.

### 2. Corrections to my own Round 2

1. **N6 was overstated.** [PE] Replay's scope does contain the BTC signals, but they don't become visible at the same moment as in Production.
   - At an equal close boundary, Replay orders the BTC signal before the primary symbol's signal (`RegressionTestWorker:557-560`), and its lookup accepts a signal generated at exactly the reference time (`ExecutionReplayScope:129-136`).
   - Production sees the BTC signal only if its analysis was committed before the primary symbol's query (`BtcMarketContextService:77-86`).
   - So the visibility-time problem (N4) also covers BTC context.
2. **R-27 left out V106.** [PE] Three kinds of record start at V106 (`V106:2-10`):
   - the signal mode;
   - the candle's ingestion source and close token;
   - the price event's ordering slot and outcome reason.

   Before V106, Production's LIVE and HISTORICAL_ONLY signals can't be told apart.
3. **R-19 timing was imprecise.** [PE]
   - Production creates the daily budget row on the first `executeBuy` *attempt* of the UTC day. The row is created before sizing and stays even when sizing then declines (`WalletAutoExecutionService:97-109, 689-715`).
   - Replay recomputes the budget at the first *signal* of each UTC day (`ShadowProductionReplayService:292-299`).
4. **R-15 and R-16 missed the price-authority dependence.** [PE]
   - Production evaluates the profit lock on the signal path only when the signal's price is authoritative. Otherwise it treats the lock as inactive (`PaperTradingService:133-137`).
   - So even when a lock is persisted as active, a signal with a non-authoritative price is not stopped from selling (`:199`) and doesn't block a progressive add (`:217`).
   - Replay's exact-price add path has no lock gate at all (`ShadowProductionReplayService:342-366`).
5. **The "silent false" was imprecise.** [PE] When the wallet declines an initial entry, it is logged (`PaperTradingService:390-391`). When it declines a progressive add, nothing is logged (`:249-255`). Neither is stored in the database.
6. **N2 needs clarifying.** [PE]
   - FIX-154's "data quality" means only that the price is positive and the 20-, 50- and 200-period EMAs are present (`MarketContextService:74-75`). It is not the candle data-quality check (`CandleDataQualityService`).
   - Signals stored before V107 have NULL quality. A NULL-quality bearish 1h signal is rejected with `1H_BEARISH_VETO` (`HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService:27-28, 48`).
   - Replay regenerates the same 1h SELL with quality true, which is allowed as a SOFT_CONFLICT entry capped at 25% (`:33-34`).
   - So for up to 3 hours after V107, Replay can open entries that Production refused.

### 3. New findings

- **N7: Replay fills one price update earlier than Production.**
  - [PE] Replay takes the fill price from the last price event it consumed at or before the candle boundary (`ExecutionPriceAuthorityService:37-43`; `ShadowProductionReplayService:126-130, 310-311`). Each price event's time is Binance's event time `E` (`BinanceKlineService:94`).
  - [PE] Production looks up the price at wall-clock time, after live protection has already applied the candle's closing message (`PaperTradingService:342-343`; `BinanceKlineService:101-111`).
  - [AI] If the closing message's `E` falls after the boundary (likely, but not measured), Replay always fills at the last update before the close, while Production fills at the close price or later.
- **N8: Stored evidence can't identify the candle-closing message.**
  - [PE] `market_price_event` has no closed flag and no candle open time (`V64:4-13`; `V89:22-25`; `V106:5-8`).
  - [PE] Production runs protection before analysis specifically for the closing message (`BinanceKlineService:101-111`), so Replay can only infer which row that was.
  - [PE] A closing message that was rejected as stale leaves no row at all (`MarketPriceEventService:53-58`).
- **N9: Derivatives also change which strategy is selected.**
  - [PE] A crowded-longs or crowded-shorts market context selects the DEFENSIVE strategy (`MarketStrategyService:43-47`).
  - [PE] When derivatives are enabled, Replay's market-context result is always UNAVAILABLE (`MarketContextService:56-58`), so Replay can never take that branch.
  - Together with N1, derivatives affect three things: strategy selection, confidence (`FinalDecisionService:222, 270-279`), and the crowding veto (`DerivativesPositioningService:75-86`).
- **N10: A processing outcome changes later decisions, not just the current one.**
  - [PE] `evaluateBuy` saves opportunity memory to `execution_opportunity` (`ExecutionIntelligenceService:2621-2623`) inside the coordinator's transaction (`SignalProcessingCoordinator:64-103`).
  - [PE] An EXPIRED signal never reaches that step (`:99`). A daily-loss throw rolls the save back (`PaperTradingService:326, 552`; coordinator `:113-131`).
  - [PE] Replay always saves it.
  - [AI] So later wake-ups and evidence scores can diverge after any such outcome.
- **N11: FIX-122's stop history is account state.**
  - [PE] Production reads the most recent STOP_LOSS sell from `wallet_trade` (`StopLossEvidenceStore:37-45`).
  - [PE] Replay uses only its own stops (`StopLossEvidenceService:43-45`; `ExecutionReplayScope:49-59`). That list starts empty, because the 3-hour warm-up never trades (`RegressionTestWorker:51`; `ShadowProductionReplayService:286-303`).
  - [PE] Replay takes the FIX-122 on/off setting from the run request, not from Production's `trading.fix122.enabled` (`StopLossEvidenceService:32, 41`; `ShadowProductionReplayService:113-116`).
- **N12: The execution decision reads Production wallet-derived state.**
  - [PE] Besides N11, three other places do this:
    - The BUY_CONTINUATION check looks up an executed BUY in `wallet_trade` (`EntryConsumptionPolicy:29-30`; `TradeExecutionValidationService:67-78`).
    - Progressive adds read `wallet_managed_position` and `wallet_trade` (`PaperTradingService:218-233`).
    - Opportunity memory is read from `execution_opportunity` (`ExecutionIntelligenceService:2617`).
  - [AI] So parity at the analysis (signal) level doesn't depend on the account, but parity at the execution-decision level does.
  - [AI] Under strict isolation (decision U-2(a) below), neither execution-decision nor lifecycle parity can be verified for any window. Even proving that Production's account was flat at the window start needs those reads.
- **N13: Production and Replay record a different position open time, which changes price authority.**
  - [PE] Production records the open time as the wall clock at execution (`PaperTradingService:412`; `WalletAutoExecutionService:755-763`).
  - [PE] Replay records the execution signal's `generatedAt` (`ShadowProductionReplayService:333`). For a 5m wake-up, that is an earlier 1m signal's time.
  - [PE] The price-authority check compares a candle's close time with the open time (`PositionPriceAuthorityPolicy:23-30`).
  - Once R-15 is built, this difference decides which signal prices can trigger an exit.
- **N14: Another shared side effect.** [PE] Through the BTC-context path, Replay increments Production's context metrics, including Micrometer counters (`BtcMarketContextService:97-100`; `SignalContextMetrics:10-32`, which has no Replay check).
- **N15: Deployment records can't reconstruct the configuration.**
  - [PE] `process.json` stores the JAR's SHA-256 and the command-line overrides. It doesn't store the commit, the external `application.yml`, or environment variables (`scripts/deploy/Deploy-Trader.ps1:84, 123-142`).
  - [PE] The evidence copy is overwritten on every deploy (`:142`). Per-release copies survive only while their release folders are kept.
- **N16: claude-docs contradict the source.** [PE] Reported as AGENTS.md requires; not updated, since that isn't authorised this round.
  - `ARCHITECTURE.md:19` and `TRADING_RULES.md:124` say FIX-154 isn't implemented.
  - `ARCHITECTURE.md:24` lists migrations only up to V106.
  - `ARCHITECTURE.md:161` says Replay writes only `*_test` tables; it also writes `fix122_evaluation` (`StopLossEvidenceStore:48-55`). The same line lists `ReplayParityValidator` as in use.

**Paths checked and found equivalent** [PE]:
- **Entry stage:** Production passes the stored decision source as the "entry stage", while Replay derives it from the allocation. `progressivePositionDecision` never reads that value (`ExecutionIntelligenceService:1032-1143`).
- **FIX-154 audit table:** writes to it are skipped during Replay (`BuyHtfAssessmentAuditStore:43-44`). Only the counters leak.
- **Signal-path lock exit:** the "triggered" flag is recomputed from price alone on every evaluation (`ProfitLockPolicy:51`). So when live protection holds a lock breach, the signal path still closes the position at the next candle close at or below the lock. This confirms the response's "label-free" item (CR-03).

### 5. Requirements for the next single complete specification

Each item needs a registry ID, current and new behaviour, Production impact, tests, logs and a rollback. Tags: (=) unchanged since my Round 2, (Δ) changed, (+) new.

**User decisions to get first**
- **U-1 (Δ) Parity target.** Either the frozen working tree (which includes FIX-154) or the JAR actually deployed for each window.
  - Mapping each window needs records linking each build to its commit, which `process.json` doesn't have (N15).
  - Either way, report the FIX-154 contradiction: AGENTS.md vs `V109:1-2` vs `TradeExecutionValidationService:93-98`.
- **U-2 (Δ) Production read boundary, with every table named.** Either:
  - **(a)** no reads of `wallet_*`, `paper_position`, `execution_opportunity`, `signal_processing_work` and similar tables. Then only analysis-level parity can be certified (N12).
  - **(b)** named, read-only, non-locking reads, used only as declared evidence that is injected or compared.
- **U-3 (=) Look-ahead.** Either reuse Production's stored context (faithful, but it inherits the look-ahead) or refuse windows that depend on recovered signals.
- **U-4 (=) Default mode** while evidence capture doesn't exist yet.
- **U-5 (=) Retirements:** FIX-11K, FIX-11Q, the pre-FIX-122 selector, and EXPERIMENTAL.
- **U-6 (=) First-release scope:** single symbol (recommended) or multi-symbol.
- **U-7 (+) Claim tiers.** Analysis parity, then execution-decision parity, then lifecycle parity, otherwise simulation. Each tier has its own eligibility rules.

**A. Baseline and governance**
- **R-01 (=)** Freeze a tree hash that includes untracked files.
- **R-02 (Δ)** Handle FIX-154 according to U-1. Add a test for the NULL-quality era (C6).
- **R-03 (=)** Reserve migration numbers after V109. Provide the table that V103 needs in the disposable test database (`db/migration/V103…java:43-52`).
- **R-04 (Δ)** Map each window to a JAR, a commit and the configuration in effect. Where that can't be reconstructed, refuse any claim that depends on version (N15).
- **R-05 (=)** Define FIX-155 or remove every reference to it.
- **R-06 (Δ)** Use documentation paths that exist, supersede `docs/REPLAY_PRODUCTION_PARITY.md:10`, and fix the claude-docs (N16) once approved.

**B. Contract, oracle and isolation**
- **R-07 (Δ) Oracle registry per tier:** for each decision, the source record, identity, as-of time, availability time, compared fields, equality rule, and the result when the record is absent. The execution tier must list its account-derived inputs (N11, N12).
- **R-08 (=) Remove Replay reads of `wallet_settings` used as decision inputs:**
  - `ShadowProductionReplayService:79, 726, 757, 777, 1012`;
  - `TradeExecutionValidationService:66, 152`;
  - `OneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver:195`.

  No locking reads (`WalletAutoExecutionService:717-719`).
- **R-09 (Δ) Inventory of external inputs and side effects, denied by default,** with a test that uses client stubs that fail on use. Cover:
  - every HTTP client;
  - mutable configuration (`wallet_settings`, the sentiment provider settings);
  - writes to shared tables (`fix122_evaluation`);
  - in-memory metrics (`Fix154Metrics`, `SignalContextMetrics`).
- **R-10 (Δ) Matrix of context inputs:** derivatives (two calls), order book (two calls), sentiment, fundamentals, BTC context and higher timeframes. For each: recompute, inject stored evidence, or refuse. Register N1, N9 and the mixed semantics of recovered signals.
- **R-11 (Δ) Qualify the window before any writes.** The qualification must not depend on Replay's own positions (for example, require price coverage across the whole window). Otherwise, hold the output in memory and persist it only after the window qualifies.
- **R-12 (Δ) Comparator.** Replace or extend `ReplayParityValidator` to add derivatives status, `data_quality_valid`, `signal_mode`, `generated_at` and `finalEntryAllowed`, and carry price-event IDs.
- **R-13 (Δ) Processing outcomes.** EXPIRED, REVIEW_REQUIRED and LOCK_RETRY_EXHAUSTED depend on what happened before (N10). Either reproduce them from `signal_processing_work.status` by skipping the business step entirely, or refuse the window.

**C. Orchestration parity**
- **R-14 (Δ) Ordering.**
  - Identify the closing message: either capture a closed flag or candle open time going forward, or declare an inference rule and refuse when it's ambiguous (N8).
  - Take each signal's visibility time from Production's `generated_at` (N4).
  - Take the same-boundary order of intervals and of BTC vs. the primary symbol from Production evidence, not from the hard-coded order (`RegressionTestWorker:557-560, 787-793`; C1).
  - Define a tie rule for LEGACY rows.
- **R-15 (Δ)** Reproduce the signal-path exit chain for every interval, including the price-authority dependence (C4).
- **R-16 (Δ)** Reproduce the progressive-add gate, including the lock gate that depends on price authority.
- **R-17 (Δ) Clocks:** analysis time, fill time (N7), the near-TP clock (`LivePositionProtectionService:256`), the UTC trading day, and the position open time (N13).
- **R-18 (=) Candle admission:** closed candles only, Production's identity check, outcomes from durable records.
- **R-19 (Δ) Wallet controls:** the 3-position cap, the daily-loss check that throws, the daily budget fixed at the first `executeBuy` attempt (C3), the daily count of new positions, and the reserve.
- **R-20 (Δ) Order book:** the opposite empty-window behaviour (LIVE signals only), its confidence effect, and the fewer-rows/more-rows cases. Either add a completeness marker or refuse.
- **R-21 (=) Sentiment:** current provider weights and the separate health rule for past reference times (`SentimentService:180-198`).
- **R-22 (Δ) FIX-122:** honour Production's flag, and handle the stop boundary at the window start (N11).
- **R-23 (=) Window end:** price events after the last signal are never processed, and an open position is valued at the last signal's price (`ShadowProductionReplayService:126-134, 370-374`).
- **R-24 (Δ) Minimum order value.** Production doesn't store the minimum it saw, and skipped harvests exist only in logs. Declare the source of truth.
- **R-25 (=) Execution expiry** (`SignalProcessingFreshness:31-48`).
- **R-26 (+) Flat starting state.** Replay's warm-up never trades. So execution-tier and lifecycle claims need proof, under U-2, that at the window start Production had:
  - no open position;
  - no BUY consumed in the last 10 minutes;
  - no stop-loss boundary affecting in-window evidence;
  - no executed or active opportunity.

  Otherwise refuse those claims.

**D. Production impact**
- **R-27 (=)** For every shared class touched: regression tests, latency and transaction analysis, and a rollback. Rewrite RPR-0.4 §6.2 to match code that already branches on Replay.

**E. Evidence model**
- **R-28 (Δ) Evidence eras:**
  - the price-source eras;
  - V106, V107 and V108;
  - the deployment hashes.

  Any PENDING row refuses the run. Excluded price events are excluded from the protection stream only (N3).
- **R-29 (=)** State whether the new evidence check extends or replaces `assertReplayEvidence`.

**F. Scope and resources**
- **R-30 (Δ) Single symbol first.** A lifecycle claim needs proof that the global limits never bound during the window, which needs the Production reads decided in U-2.
- **R-31 (=) Resources:** Replay shares the JVM and the 15-connection pool with the trader (`application.yml:17`). Define how quickly cancellation takes effect, and test that Production freshness holds while Replay runs.

**G. API, UI and retirements**
- **R-32 (=)** Set the default mode per U-4, specify the API changes, and keep the archive endpoints compatible.

## OBJECTIONS

**Material** (to the response treated as the requirements basis):
1. The requirement to refuse before any writes is still dropped. RPR-0.4's eligibility rule depends on positions that are only known after Replay executes (R-11).
2. The look-ahead choice still isn't required (U-3). Recovered signals mixing "unavailable" with current data make this stronger.
3. The order-book inversion isn't registered. Neither are the cases where stored rows are fewer or more than what Production saw (R-20).
4. The wallet-read rule contradicts itself, and it is now a decision about which parity tiers can be claimed (N12; U-2, U-7).
5. CR-06's options are incomplete: injecting stored evidence is missing, and N9 isn't registered.
6. CR-03 is incomplete: C4, N7, N10 and N13 are missing, along with the gaps from Round 2.
7. CR-07 doesn't mention that FIX-154 is already in the shared entry gate, or that configuration can't be reconstructed from deployment records (N15).
8. CR-05 doesn't address identifying the closing message (N8) or the V106 era (C2).

**Non-material:**
- §4 names the wrong check: the one that throws is the daily-loss check, not the budget.
- §4's RegimeStateService reset is moot (agreed with Codex).
- `docs/REPLAY_PRODUCTION_PARITY.md:10` contradicts the code.
- The documentation paths named in AGENTS.md don't exist.
- claude-docs are stale (N16).

**On Codex's objections:** I accept all eleven. I correct one finding (equal-time ordering is deterministic, but can't be certified) and extend four (order book, recovered derivatives, wallet reads, expiry).

## UNKNOWNS
1. Which JAR and configuration were deployed for each window; whether FIX-153 and FIX-154 are deployed; whether old release folders and their `process.json` survive.
2. How far Binance's `E` for closing messages trails the boundary, and how `liveCloseToGenerationMs` is distributed. These decide how large N4 and N7 are.
3. How often a lock breach held by live protection was then closed by a signal-path PROFIT_LOCK exit.
4. How often EXPIRED, REVIEW_REQUIRED, LOCK_RETRY_EXHAUSTED, PENDING_REPLACED and ANALYSIS_EXPIRED occurred.
5. Whether the stored context fields are enough to inject, field by field.
6. Whether Binance's open-interest and funding history line up with what Production saw (ER-5 and ER-6 don't settle this).
7. The date each era began (V106, V107, V108, and the price-source changes).
8. How many order-book rows were dropped; this is recorded only in WARN logs.
9. Nothing was compiled, run or queried.

## VERDICT
RPR-0.4 stays rejected, and the response still isn't a complete basis for the next revision. It drops the pre-write contradiction and leaves the look-ahead and wallet-read decisions open. It also misses the order-book inversion, the closing-message evidence gap, the derivatives effect on strategy selection, and the fact that execution decisions read Production wallet-derived state. R-01 to R-35, together with user decisions U-1 to U-7, are what the next single specification has to cover. This verdict doesn't authorise any change.

Nothing was written. Plan mode is active, this session has no write or ExitPlanMode tool, and documentation changes (N16) aren't authorised this round.

This file contains permanent project instructions.

Codex MUST NOT modify AGENTS.md unless the user explicitly requests

- codex-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md

## Mandatory Documentation Maintenance

Documentation updates are part of the implementation and are NOT optional.

After every implementation, Codex MUST determine whether the change affects
architecture, trading behavior, operational behavior, or previous technical
decisions.

### Architecture changes
If the implementation changes any of the following:
- component responsibilities
- data flow
- database/table ownership
- event flow
- WebSocket usage
- service dependencies
- application boundaries
- ingestion architecture
- Replay/Production architecture

Codex MUST update:

docs/ARCHITECTURE.md

The architecture documentation must describe the FINAL implemented state,
not only what was originally planned.

### Every completed fix/change
Codex MUST update:

docs/CHANGELOG_AI.md

Include:
- date
- problem
- root cause
- solution
- files/classes changed
- database impact
- Production impact
- Replay impact

### Trading behavior changes
Codex MUST update:

docs/TRADING_RULES.md

### Investigation findings
Important investigation findings and rejected approaches MUST be recorded in:

docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md

## Definition of Done

A task is NOT COMPLETE until all required documentation has been updated.

Before finishing a task, Codex MUST explicitly check:
"Does this change require documentation updates?"

Do not wait for the user to ask for documentation updates.


## Trade investigations
When asked to investigate a trade:

- Confirm the application, environment, symbol, and trade ID.
- Use configured database and SSH connections without displaying secrets.
- Use read-only, bounded queries and targeted log searches.
- Read the actual schema before choosing tables or columns.
- Reconstruct the timeline: candle, signal, decision, entry, protection,
  and exit. Display timestamps in KSA (UTC+03:00), interpreting stored
  timestamps as UTC.
- Trace the relevant code and configuration for each decision.
- Check data freshness, entry quality, strategy, risk/reward, fees,
  stop-loss, and exit reason where applicable.
- Assess the entry using only information available at that moment.
  Use subsequent price action separately to evaluate the outcome.
- Distinguish a valid losing trade from a logic defect, stale data,
  execution delay, configuration issue, or missing evidence.
- Check whether the behavior can be reproduced in Replay.
- Support findings with record IDs, log timestamps, and code references.
- State uncertainties; do not invent a cause.
- Do not change code, configuration, services, or production data during
  an investigation unless the user requests a fix.

Report:
1. What happened.
2. Why the system entered and exited.
3. Whether the behavior followed the intended rules.
4. Confirmed defects and missing evidence.
5. Recommended correction and regression checks, if needed.

## Current architecture direction (pending implementation)

The current priority is to remove the trader's runtime dependency on the
collector. This is a design decision, not an implemented/deployed change.

- Target runtime: trader-owned Binance kline ingestion and local
  `crypto_ai.candle` writes with `shared-market.mode=OFF`.
- The collector remains independent in `crypto_ai_v2`; the trader must not
  connect to, read from, or write collector stream/cursor tables at runtime.
- Do not run direct ingestion and the shared-market executors together.
- Applied shared-market tables and Flyway history are historical evidence; do
  not drop, reset, or edit them merely to enable the new path.
- `V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` still requires
  `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event` for a fresh Flyway install. Treat
  trader-only bootstrap as a release blocker until a forward, tested installer
  policy exists. Never edit an applied Flyway migration.

## Local-candle implementation safety

When implementing or reviewing the local-candle design:

- Keep `shared-market.fix142.enabled=false`,
  `shared-market.fix142.acknowledgment-enabled=false`, and scheduled analysis
  disabled for the OFF-mode cutover unless a reviewed change explicitly says
  otherwise.
- Preserve the input -> protection -> observer -> close-dispatch transaction
  order. Candle close analysis must not run on the Binance ingestion thread.
- A closed candle needs durable/recoverable analysis admission; an in-memory
  event/queue alone loses work across a crash. Recovery must distinguish fresh
  execution-capable work from historical-only analysis.
- Candle updates must be monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or
  overwrite a closed candle. Publish close work only for an atomic
  open-to-closed transition.
- A WebSocket reconnect can occur at a candle-close boundary and Binance may
  not replay that final closed-kline event. Candle recovery is automatic: use
  an exact authoritative Binance REST candle only after exchange-time
  finality and the measured live-close grace. It may either finalise a matching
  existing `closed=0` row or insert a wholly absent identity inside the active
  data-quality window. A recovered open row preserves provenance; an inserted
  gap row uses `BACKFILL` provenance. Neither path may alter a completed row,
  create a close-transition token, dispatch analysis, create a live signal,
  or execute a trade. The recovery must have durable retry/audit evidence,
  bounded rate-limit handling, health visibility, and no manual repair step.
- Automation direction: no pending candle or analysis outcome may require
  manual approval, manual database repair, or a persisted trading fence.
  Reconciliation must retry and resolve automatically, or age out to a durable
  explicit outcome with a visible reason. This does not permit unverified data
  to masquerade as valid or force an entry: a genuine strategy/risk veto or
  unavailable authoritative data remains non-executable evidence, not a
  silently stuck pending signal.
- Gap backfill must be a pure insert-if-absent operation. It must never use a
  duplicate-key update that could finalise or alter a live row which appeared
  while the authoritative REST request was in progress. Recovery candidates
  are bounded by the active quality window and terminal audit outcomes are not
  retried. Each candle/work path must write one durable terminal analysis
  outcome; transient failures remain retryable and write no terminal outcome.
- Any future universal trading-mutation barrier is an in-memory drain
  participant only. Outside an active drain it must not block, delay, queue,
  or persistently fence fresh trading, signals, or protection. It may reject
  new mutations only while draining and must then wait for admitted mutations
  to finish before shutdown.
- Historical-only recovery must be structurally unable to reach execution,
  wallet, position, order, or executable-work services. Quarantined work with
  possible financial effects may be concluded automatically only from proven
  durable effect evidence (`EFFECT_COMMITTED` or `EFFECT_ABSENT`); unknown
  atomicity remains a release blocker. Binance REST rate-limit/ban handling
  must be shared by reconciliation, clock sync, bootstrap, and other REST
  callers.
- Enforce freshness from persisted candle close time before analysis and again
  before execution. Historical-only signals must be explicitly marked and
  excluded from live-latency monitoring.
- Price protection must receive the Binance observation timestamp and validate
  age/order inside the protection transaction. Paper-trading effects and their
  accepted price-event state must commit atomically; any future live-exchange
  effect needs a separate idempotent design.
- Do not add manual approval, persisted review fences, or database locks that
  block new analysis, signals, entries, or protection unless the user changes
  that direction. In-process coordination and normal database constraints are
  distinct from persisted trading fences.
- OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must not query or write retired shared-market
  tables. Existing `REVIEW_REQUIRED` history must not block OFF-mode work.
- Keep production and Replay evidence aligned. Replay must fail a parity claim
  for unresolved direct price-event evidence, while deliberate exclusions stay
  excluded.

## Delivery discipline

- Treat the local-candle architecture as not deployment-ready until its
  migration/bootstrap policy, concurrency, crash/restart, stale-input,
  Replay-parity, and Java 21/MySQL validation gates pass.
- Do not deploy or change production configuration/data unless the user
  explicitly requests it.
- Protection coverage for open positions during application replacement remains
  an unresolved release gate. Do not represent the local-candle design as
  deployment-ready until it is implemented or conclusively disproven.

## FIX-154: pending higher-timeframe BUY validation

FIX-154 is a separate, not-yet-implemented design. Do not merge it into the
local-candle change or describe it as deployed.

Purpose: a historical bearish 1h signal may block or delay a current 1m BUY
only when its own evidence is fresh, data-quality-valid, and sufficiently
strong. A historical `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` must remain investigatory
evidence, not a higher-timeframe validity gate; the current 1m signal's own
BTC protection remains unchanged.

- Score usable bearish 1h evidence into `NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`, or
  `HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE` using closed-candle local indicators.
- A hard veto requires strong 1h evidence plus fresh, quality-valid bearish
  5m confirmation. Stale, invalid, or unknown historical quality can never
  hard-veto.
- Persist `trade_signal.data_quality_valid` from
  `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` for every new signal. Historical NULL
  means unknown/invalid; never backfill it to true.
- A confirmed 1h recovery may release only a soft conflict and only with fresh,
  quality-valid, non-bearish 5m evidence.
- Preserve the legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` behavior: the new data-quality gate
  applies to the new hard-veto/recovery paths, not that legacy fallback.
- Add immutable BUY-evaluation audit evidence; maintain Replay parity and
  prevent future-candle or wall-clock look-ahead.

# MANDATORY PRE-IMPLEMENTATION CHANGE CONTRACT

This section extends the existing governance. It does not replace any earlier
project, investigation, architecture, trading, safety, or documentation rule.

For any material code change, implementation is forbidden until the team has
produced and approved the same exact revision of:

`agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION.md`

The specification is the implementation contract and MUST contain, at minimum:

1. Business objective and the exact agreed business line/flow.
2. Confirmed root cause and evidence.
3. In-scope and out-of-scope boundaries.
4. Complete class/file change registry (modify/create/delete/rename/config/
   migration/test/documentation).
5. Class-by-class and material method-by-method current behavior -> agreed new
   behavior, reason, inputs/outputs, side effects, error handling, concurrency,
   transactionality, Replay impact and Production impact.
6. Mandatory logging registry covering all materially affected areas. Logs must
   make the business flow and decisions reconstructable without exposing
   credentials/secrets. Include relevant correlation/trade/symbol/interval/
   candle/test identifiers and respect the project's UTC/KSA rules.
7. Database, configuration and trading-behavior registries, explicitly stating
   NONE when there is no impact.
8. Test and validation registry, including positive, negative, regression,
   failure/recovery, concurrency where relevant, Replay, and Production-safe
   validation.
9. Risks, rollback plan, observability plan and documentation registry.
10. Master implementation registry assigning an ID to every authorized change,
    log, DB/config item, test and documentation update.

No source/config/database/trading change may be implemented unless it is
registered in the approved specification. No unrelated refactoring or cleanup
may be hidden inside an approved fix.

## Dual-agent specification approval

The SAME exact specification revision must be fully reviewed and explicitly
approved by BOTH agents:

- Codex: APPROVED
- Claude: APPROVED
- Unresolved material disagreements: 0

There is no majority vote and no fixed review-iteration limit. Any material
revision invalidates both prior approvals and requires a complete re-review by
both agents. Even after dual approval, implementation remains NOT AUTHORIZED
until the user explicitly approves that exact revision.

## Code implementation direction

After explicit user approval:

- Codex is the implementation owner.
- Claude is the mandatory independent code reviewer.
- Claude reviews the COMPLETE implementation, not only the latest patch.
- Claude comments/rejects; Codex corrects or disputes with technical evidence;
  Claude re-reviews the complete updated implementation.
- Repeat for as many iterations as required. There is no fixed iteration cap.
- Codex cannot self-approve its implementation and the Coordinator cannot
  overrule a material Claude objection.
- Any implementation-code change after Claude approval invalidates that
  approval and requires another complete Claude review.
- A material deviation from the approved specification stops implementation,
  reopens dual-agent design review, creates a new specification revision, and
  requires renewed user approval.

## Post-implementation registry reconciliation

Before technical acceptance, reconcile the actual implementation against the
approved master registry. Every planned item must be accounted for and every
actual change must map to an approved registry ID.

Acceptance requires at minimum:

- missing registered changes: NONE
- unregistered implementation changes: NONE
- unresolved material review findings: 0
- required logging implemented and reviewed
- required tests/validation completed with evidence
- Replay/Production parity reviewed where applicable
- required documentation updates completed
- Claude final full-code review: APPROVED
- Codex final verification of the exact Claude-approved state: PASSED

# Replay / Production Business-Logic-Parity Redesign

**Revision:** RPR-0.4-DRAFT  
**Status:** DRAFT ƒ?" NOT APPROVED ƒ?" IMPLEMENTATION FORBIDDEN  
**Created:** 2026-10-10  
**Scope owner:** Codex implementation / Claude independent review

## 1. Business objective

Make manual Replay reproduce the current paper-trading Production business flow
as closely as durable historical evidence permits, while remaining completely
isolated from Production trading state and wallet data.

The exact intended flow is:

```text
historical candle + historical context + canonical accepted price events
  -> shared Production indicator/signal/decision policies
  -> replay-local virtual wallet/account state
  -> shared sizing/allocation/entry revalidation policies
  -> replay-local position lifecycle
  -> shared TP/SL/profit-lock/continuation/exit policies
  -> replay output + explicit parity certificate
```

Replay must never write `wallet_*`, `paper_position`, `trade_signal`,
`signal_processing_work`, or any other Production trading table.

The result is not a claim that historical Java scheduling, MySQL lock timing,
network delay, external Binance availability, or the historical Production
wallet balance can be reproduced. It is a verified **business-logic parity**
claim only when all required market/decision evidence exists. Otherwise Replay
remains available as a labelled simulation and may not claim parity.

## 2. Confirmed root cause and evidence

The 2026-10-10 source audit established the following:

1. Signal, entry, normal-exit, continuation, profit-lock, and near-TP policy
   use several shared Production services.
2. `ShadowProductionReplayService` separately owns replay wallet cash,
   positions, daily budget, persistence, price-loop lifecycle, and result
   labels. It starts at `INITIAL_CAPITAL=10000`, runs one symbol/position, and
   does not reproduce Production wallet/global controls.
3. A run is labelled `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` when at least one accepted price
   event exists, rather than proving coverage for every interval while a
   position was open.
4. Historical derivatives information is not persisted;
   `DerivativesPositioningService.evaluateHistorical()` returns UNAVAILABLE.
   Historical order-book evaluation can likewise be unavailable when snapshots
   were not retained.
5. Replay-only FIX-11K and FIX-11Q observers, and the pre-FIX-122 UI revision,
   have no current Production equivalent and must not contaminate parity runs.
6. Current Replay reads `WalletSettingsRepository` directly and its near-TP
   path can call `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy`, which can request a current
   live Binance minimum notional. Both violate replay account/external-input
   isolation.

## 3. Boundaries

### In scope

- Replay input/evidence qualification and run-level parity status.
- Replay-local virtual wallet state, wallet controls, positions, executions,
  daily statistics, and global limit reproduction.
- Canonical price-event coverage qualification and event ordering.
- Historical context-evidence qualification for derivatives and order book.
- Removal of approved retired Replay-only business/experiment behaviour.
- Parity certificate, diagnostics, migrations, tests, documentation.

### Out of scope

- Any Production strategy change, sizing change, exit change, or wallet change.
- Live-exchange orders, fills, fees, slippage, or order-book execution modelling.
- Replaying database locks, thread scheduling, JVM pauses, WebSocket transport,
  external REST failures, or transaction retries as historical facts.
- FIX-153, FIX-154, and FIX-155 trading-policy changes.
- Deleting Production evidence or existing historical Replay/archive rows.

## 4. Contractual modes

| Mode | Purpose | Missing evidence behaviour | Claim allowed |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` | Reproduce Production rules using an explicitly selected virtual Replay account and complete market/decision evidence. | Refuse the run before execution. | Business-logic parity. |
| `SIMULATION` | Manual what-if/research run. | Continue but persist all approximation reasons. | Simulation only. |
| `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` | Explicit, separately approved research such as FIX-155. | Per experiment contract. | Never Production parity. |

The default Administration Replay action must be `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY`; the UI
must make `SIMULATION` and `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` explicit opt-in choices.

## 5. Business-logic-parity eligibility

A `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` run must fail before generated signals or shadow wallet
state are written unless all of the following hold:

1. Every requested candle identity is closed and within the quality contract.
2. A complete immutable `ReplayAccountProfile` is supplied: starting virtual
   USDT, reserve, daily budget, daily loss state, maximum-new-position/global
   limits, and wallet settings. It is copied to replay-only tables at run start
   and is never read from or written to a Production wallet table.
3. The selected replay universe is declared. The virtual account applies
   global limits across every symbol included in that run; no claim is made
   that it reproduces the historical Production wallet's other positions.
4. The selected profile contains versioned execution constraints for relevant
   symbols, including minimum executable notional. Replay never fetches a
   current exchange constraint while evaluating historical events.
5. Every canonical direct price observation required from the first replay
   position open through its terminal close is represented by accepted
   `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` evidence, with no unresolved `PENDING` row in the whole
   consumed range.
6. Required historical order-book evidence exists whenever the feature was
   enabled for the corresponding Production decision.
7. Required historical derivatives evidence exists whenever derivatives were
   enabled and could change confidence or entry authority. Until durable
   derivatives persistence exists, affected requests must be refused in this
   mode rather than silently treating UNAVAILABLE as equivalent.
8. The exact Production configuration/version fingerprint is available for the
   requested period, or all version-dependent decision paths are marked
   unavailable and the mode is refused.

`SIMULATION` may use configured defaults or fixed starting capital but must
store each deviation in its output. It may never be displayed as parity.

### 5.1 Wallet isolation boundary

Replay never reads Production `wallet_asset`, `wallet_settings`,
`wallet_trade`, `wallet_managed_position`, `paper_position`, daily-statistics,
or wallet-snapshot rows as a decision input. The existing data does not retain
complete historical wallet state and is deliberately irrelevant to a Replay
account. A user-selected/versioned `ReplayAccountProfile` is the only account
input. This preserves safe isolation while exercising the same pure sizing and
position-management formulas.

The profile also contains symbol execution constraints used by shared
paper-order guards. `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy` must be split into a pure
minimum-notional evaluator plus a Production-only exchange-constraint provider;
Replay receives immutable profile values and may not issue Binance HTTP.

## 6. Agreed behaviour changes

### 6.1 Replay starting state

Replace hard-coded starting capital with an immutable `ReplayStartingState`.
It is seeded from the selected, versioned `ReplayAccountProfile` and stored
under the replay run ID before processing begins. Shadow wallet/position
services consume only this replay-local state and replay tables.

The run-start state must include profile ID/version, creation timestamp, version/config fingerprint,
virtual USDT/asset balances, daily-budget state, daily loss state, virtual
managed positions, virtual paper positions, replay idempotency keys, and a
versioned wallet/settings/configuration fingerprint. It may not be refreshed
from Production while a run is executing.

### 6.2 Shared paper-wallet business authority

Extract or complete pure shared policies for:

- initial and progressive sizing;
- reserve, daily budget, daily-loss, maximum-new-position, and global
  maximum-open-position controls;
- buy/sell/partial-harvest quantity and cost-basis calculations;
- position average entry, target/stop updates, profit-lock state, and near-TP
  state transitions;
- idempotency-key interpretation.

Production retains its current transactional repositories. Replay receives
replay-local adapters implementing the same policy inputs/outputs. No policy
may branch on `replay=true` to produce a different business decision.

### 6.3 Price evidence

Replace the boolean/label derived from ƒ?oany event existsƒ?? with a coverage
result containing requested interval, covered interval, gaps, accepted event
count, rejected/unresolved count, and every position-open interval requiring
protection.

`BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` rejects incomplete coverage. `SIMULATION` may use
signal-price fallback only with the persisted reason `PRICE_PATH_INCOMPLETE`.

### 6.4 Historical decision evidence

Persist versioned derivatives and order-book observations required by
Production, or mark affected historical intervals ineligible for business-logic
parity. Replay must use the same normalized evaluator and only evidence at or
before the replay clock. It must never call live Binance endpoints.

### 6.5 Event ordering and time

Use canonical UTC `observed_at` and event IDs for price ordering. Define a
single deterministic tie order matching Productionƒ?Ts committed sequence:

1. accepted price protection event;
2. resulting position/wallet state;
3. closed-candle analysis signal;
4. signal-driven position action.

If durable evidence cannot establish the required tie order, business-logic parity
must refuse the run. Replay timestamps remain presentation-neutral UTC;
KSA conversion belongs only to UI/reporting.

### 6.6 Retired Replay-only behaviour

The following are **candidates for removal**, subject to complete-reference
review and approval. They must be removed from baseline parity runs now; any
future research requires an independently approved module and run mode.

| Registry ID | Candidate | Proposed disposition |
|---|---|---|
| RPR-RM-01 | `DefensiveRiskReductionReplayObserver` / FIX-11K | Remove source, controller/UI/output tables only after archive/export compatibility is proved. |
| RPR-RM-02 | `OneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver` / FIX-11Q | Remove source, controller/UI/output tables only after archive/export compatibility is proved. |
| RPR-RM-03 | pre-FIX-122 replay revision selector | Remove the UI/API option and old comparison path; Production-current FIX-122 remains the only parity rule. |
| RPR-RM-04 | `EXPERIMENTAL` logic mode if no approved user-facing experiment consumes it | Remove after exact reference search; otherwise retain as an isolated framework with no baseline access. |

`ReplayDataSource.DATABASE` versus `DATASET` is not retired business logic:
both must remain only if a test proves equal outputs. It is a data-access
implementation choice, not an old Production-rule selector.

## 7. Implementation change registry

No implementation item is authorized until this exact revision is approved.

| ID | Area | Required change |
|---|---|---|
| RPR-01 | run contract | Add persisted mode, evidence status, account profile/state ID, version/config fingerprint, and refusal reason to the replay run. |
| RPR-02 | replay account schema/service | Add `ReplayAccountProfile` and immutable run-start-state tables. Seed virtual state only from the selected Replay profile. |
| RPR-03 | wallet authority | Extract/reuse pure paper-wallet policy and provide Production/replay persistence adapters; remove direct Replay reads of `WalletSettingsRepository`. |
| RPR-03a | execution constraints | Split minimum-notional evaluation from live exchange constraint acquisition; inject immutable profile constraints into Replay and prohibit Replay Binance HTTP. |
| RPR-04 | shadow replay | Replace fixed capital/local single-position assumptions with replay-local state and multi-symbol/global-limit coordinator. |
| RPR-05 | price evidence | Add interval coverage validator and replace the current any-event exact-price label. |
| RPR-06 | historical context | Add versioned persistence/lookup/qualification for order book and derivatives, or explicit parity refusal. |
| RPR-07 | timing/order | Persist/use deterministic committed event ordering and reject unknown ordering for parity runs. |
| RPR-08 | certificate | Add immutable parity certificate containing field comparisons, evidence counts, deviations, and final claim. |
| RPR-09 | removals | Execute only the approved RPR-RM entries, including migration/archive compatibility. |
| RPR-10 | UI/API | Make mode explicit; default to business-logic parity; display refusal/deviation/certificate details. |
| RPR-11 | tests | Add unit, integration, MySQL, complete scenario, no-look-ahead, and Production/Replay comparison tests below. |
| RPR-12 | docs | Update architecture, rules, changelog, investigation, operational runbook, and parity contract. |

## 8. Error handling, concurrency, and transactions

- Replay-state creation is isolated and must be consistent for one profile version.
- Business-logic-parity evidence validation happens before replay execution; no
  partial shadow run may be labelled a parity run.
- Replay output writes are transactionally isolated by `test_run_id`; a failed
  run records its failure and certificate status without changing Production.
- Replay must not lock Production wallet/position rows for business mutation.
- Replay-profile/state reads must not expose credentials and must log only IDs,
  UTC times, symbol counts, mode, and evidence result.
- Cancellation preserves isolated run evidence and cannot leave a completed
  parity certificate.

## 9. Logging and observability registry

| ID | Event | Mandatory fields |
|---|---|---|
| RPR-L-01 | virtual starting state created/refused | runId, mode, profile ID/version, virtual balances/position counts, config fingerprint, reason |
| RPR-L-02 | evidence qualified/refused | runId, symbol(s), from/to UTC, candle/price/orderbook/derivatives status, gap counts |
| RPR-L-03 | shadow execution decision | runId, symbol, signal/candle ID, decision code, shared-policy source, replay state ID |
| RPR-L-04 | parity mismatch | runId, field, Production value, replay value, evidence reference, severity |
| RPR-L-05 | certificate completed | runId, claim, matched/mismatched field counts, simulation deviations, duration |
| RPR-L-06 | retired path blocked | runId, removed/experimental feature ID, requested source, outcome |

## 10. Database/configuration/trading impact

- **Database:** additive replay account/snapshot/certificate/evidence schema and any
  required durable historical derivatives observations. Applied migrations are
  never edited. Archive migration/retention is mandatory before deletion of
  retired Replay-only output tables.
- **Configuration:** add replay mode/evidence policy only. Production defaults
  and trading configuration remain unchanged.
- **Trading behaviour:** NONE. Production is read-only to this feature and no
  live/paper Production decision, wallet mutation, position, or protection
  rule may change.
- **Replay behaviour:** baseline becomes evidence-qualified business-logic parity
  or explicitly labelled simulation; retired experimental paths are removed or
  isolated only as approved above.

## 11. Test and validation registry

| ID | Required proof |
|---|---|
| RPR-T-01 | The same profile inputs produce equal Production/replay sizing, reserve, daily budget, daily-loss, and global-position-limit decisions through shared policies. |
| RPR-T-01a | Replay refuses `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` when no immutable replay account profile is selected; it must never silently use a Production wallet row. |
| RPR-T-02 | A missing, unresolved, rejected, or coverage-gapped price event refuses business-logic parity and labels simulation correctly. |
| RPR-T-03 | Price-event/signal same-time ordering matches Production for TP, SL, profit lock, normal exit, and signal SELL. |
| RPR-T-04 | End-to-end initial BUY, progressive add, target extension, rebase, stop, profit lock, near-TP partial harvest, terminal exit, and P&L lifecycle match field-for-field. |
| RPR-T-05 | Historical order book/derivatives use only as-of persisted data; missing evidence refuses parity; no live HTTP occurs. |
| RPR-T-05a | Replay never reads a Production wallet settings row or calls a Binance minimum-notional endpoint; profile values drive the same pure minimum-order decision. |
| RPR-T-06 | Multi-symbol global wallet/max-position test proves no isolated-symbol bypass. |
| RPR-T-07 | Cancellation/error tests prove Production tables remain unchanged and certificates cannot falsely pass. |
| RPR-T-08 | MySQL migration and transaction tests use a disposable database. |
| RPR-T-09 | Dataset and database replay inputs produce equivalent output for the same qualified evidence, or one path is removed. |
| RPR-T-10 | Removal tests prove baseline Replay cannot invoke FIX-11K, FIX-11Q, or pre-FIX-122 paths. |
| RPR-T-11 | Real historical parity suite compares selected known Production windows and produces stored certificates. |

## 12. Risks, rollback, and acceptance

- Risk: historical data required for verified parity may not exist. Mitigation:
  refuse parity and use clearly labelled simulation; do not invent evidence.
- Risk: snapshot schema increases storage. Mitigation: bounded retention and
  archive strategy, never deletion of active/investigation evidence.
- Risk: retiring observers breaks historical UI/archive reads. Mitigation:
  migration and archive/export compatibility tests before removal.
- Rollback: disable new business-logic-parity mode and retain existing archived
  output; Production requires no rollback because it is never mutated.

Acceptance requires: all registry IDs reconciled; no unregistered code change;
all test evidence green; disposable MySQL validation; complete source review by
Claude; Codex verification of the exact reviewed revision; and explicit user
authorization after both agents approve this same document.

# RPR-0.4 independent-review response

**Date:** 2026-10-10  
**Status:** Analysis response only ƒ?" RPR-0.4 remains rejected ƒ?" no implementation authorised  
**Inputs reviewed:** Coordinator run `20261010-095027`, Claude rounds 1ƒ?"3 and Codex cross-examination.

## 1. Conclusion

The independent review correctly found that RPR-0.4 is a useful direction but
is not a complete implementation contract. It must not be implemented as
written. This response does not amend or approve RPR-0.4; it records the
required shape of a replacement revision (RPR-0.5 or later).

## 2. Accepted material findings and required correction

| ID | Accepted finding | Required correction in the next complete specification |
|---|---|---|
| CR-01 | The change registry is too high-level to reconcile implementation. | Supply a registry with one ID per artifact: exact file/class/method, before/after behaviour, configuration key, database migration/table/column/index, log event/level/context, tests, documentation, rollback and Production/Replay effect. |
| CR-02 | The proposal changes shared Production classes even if the intended business-policy change is Replay-only. | Replace the claim ƒ?oProduction is read-only/no rollbackƒ?? with a per-item impact statement. Any shared extraction or evidence capture must have Production regression tests, transaction/latency analysis and a code rollback path. No intended production trading-policy change is permitted. |
| CR-03 | Current Replay misses production orchestration, not only wallet state. | Register the close-message/price-event lineage, label-free profit-lock exit, advisory stop-loss, SELL/add suppression during an active profit lock, progressive-add gate, analysis/fill clocks, historical-only/skipped candles, same-boundary higher-timeframe visibility and daily-budget timing. Each must be either implemented for parity, classified as an allowed divergence, or cause a parity refusal. |
| CR-04 | ƒ?oParityƒ?? has no usable oracle after the virtual account diverges from Production. | Define separate outcomes: (a) **decision parity**, compared with stored as-of Production decision evidence; (b) **lifecycle parity**, only for an authorised matching virtual starting state; and (c) **simulation**, useful output that must never claim parity. Classify scheduling/concurrency differences explicitly rather than silently accepting them. |
| CR-05 | Evidence qualification is incomplete and incorrectly treats deliberate exclusions as failures. | Define an era boundary and eligible evidence set. A parity run must use the same accepted/applied price observations Production could use; `NON_CANONICAL` and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` remain deliberate exclusions, not missing evidence. Require as-of candle identity/provenance, signal mode/outcome, configuration/version, sentiment availability time and applicable order-book/derivative evidence. A gap definition belongs in simulation diagnostics; parity refuses only when required accepted evidence is absent. |
| CR-06 | Default `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` is not currently reachable for evidence eras with derivatives/configuration history missing. | Do not make an impossible mode the default. The replacement must either (1) register forward capture of the missing evidence and make parity available only from that capture date, or (2) require the caller to choose `SIMULATION` explicitly and reject a parity request with exact reasons. |
| CR-07 | The base revision, migration sequencing and deployment facts conflict. | Freeze an exact Git commit/diff identity before design approval. Reconcile the observed migration/code state with AGENTS.md and actual Flyway history using read-only evidence. The replacement spec must state the base commit, migration numbers reserved, merge order, target database compatibility and the outcome if an expected table/migration is absent. |
| CR-08 | Multi-symbol/global-budget ordering is not designed. | Either restrict the first parity scope to one virtual account/symbol with an explicit refusal for multi-symbol runs, or register deterministic cross-symbol scheduling and global-limit claim ordering in `ExecutionReplayScope` with tests. |
| CR-09 | Replay-only removals are product decisions, not automatic parity repairs. | Do not remove FIX-11K, FIX-11Q, the pre-FIX122 selector, or `EXPERIMENTAL` mode in the parity change unless their callers, persisted data and user-facing workflows are inventoried and the user approves their retirement. Until then, isolate them from baseline parity and make their outcome clearly experimental. |

## 3. Additional source findings to preserve in the next scope

The independent review identified source-backed gaps that must become registry
items rather than prose aspirations:

- Replay currently can analyse a `closed=0` candle and use an earlier candle
  snapshot. A parity run needs durable candle identity/finality checks.
- The live close path and Replay do not necessarily see the same 1m decision
  at a candle boundary. Event lineage, not a loose timestamp sort, must define
  the replay ordering rule.
- Production can subsequently close an active profit lock without rechecking
  labels, while current Replay may retain the position. It also has an advisory
  signal-path stop-loss that Replay does not invoke.
- Historical derivatives/order-book/sentiment evidence may be unavailable or
  have only event time rather than record/availability time. Unknown evidence
  must produce a diagnostic refusal for parity, not a no-look-ahead claim.
- A Replay account must never read Production `wallet_*` state. The forbidden
  scope must name the Production tables precisely while allowing Replay-owned
  test/result tables where approved.
- A manual replay run executes in the same JVM and database pool as the trader.
  Its resource limits and cancellation behaviour must protect Production
  freshness and must be tested.

## 4. Non-material corrections to include

- Include the current configuration values that affect sizing and daily limits,
  including `max-open-positions` and `max-daily-loss-percent`.
- Define behaviour when the Production daily-budget check would fail or throw.
- Clear `RegimeStateService` replay state deterministically after every run.
- State whether any new Replay evidence pre-write extends or replaces the
  current pending-evidence guard.
- Document that current paper trades use zero fees, so parity is not a claim
  about exchange-net profitability.
- Test cancellation/worker completion, two-request admission behaviour,
  curated and random/incident windows, look-ahead, and refusal-rate reporting.

## 5. Required next review package

Before another document gate, provide one exact revised specification and no
implementation. It must include:

1. A frozen source baseline and migration/deployment reconciliation.
2. The full artifact registry described in CR-01.
3. The three parity levels and their explicit oracles/refusal conditions.
4. The evidence-era and availability-time model.
5. The single-symbol-first or deterministic multi-symbol decision.
6. A user decision table for each proposed Replay-only retirement.
7. A complete test/validation/rollback/documentation plan.

Until then, `RPR-0.4-DRAFT` remains **REJECTED** and no source, database,
configuration or deployment change is authorised.

===== codex-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md =====
   1: # Crypto AI Trader Г?" Architecture
   2: 
   3: Status labels used in this document:
   4: 
   5: - **Current** Г?" present in the repository today.
   6: - **Pending** Г?" approved design direction, not implemented or deployed.
   7: - **Legacy/shared** Г?" existing shared-market path retained as historical evidence
   8:   while the trader moves to local-candle ownership.
   9: 
  10: ## Current release status
  11: 
  12: **FIX-153 is implemented in source but is NOT APPROVED FOR PRODUCTION.**
  13: It moves the trader toward collector-free, trader-owned local candle ingestion
  14: in `shared-market.mode=OFF`. Java 21, disposable-MySQL, crash/race, Windows
  15: two-process, soak, OFF-mode and Replay-parity proof gates remain open.
  16: 
  17: FIX-154 is a separate pending higher-timeframe design. It is not implemented
  18: and is not part of FIX-153 deployment scope.
  19: 
  20: ## System purpose
  21: 
  22: The application is a Spring Boot cryptocurrency trader that analyses Binance
  23: market data, persists candles and technical indicators, creates trade signals,
  24: and performs paper-wallet execution and position protection. Production and
  25: Replay are intended to use equivalent persisted market evidence.
  26: 
  27: ## Runtime and data ownership
  28: 
  29: | Area | Current ownership | Target direction |
  30: |---|---|---|
  31: | Trader database | `crypto_ai` | Remains the trader's only runtime database. |
  32: | Collector database | `crypto_ai_v2` | Collector remains separate and is not read/written by the trader at runtime. |
  33: | Candle ingestion | In OFF mode, trader-owned Binance ingestion writes local `crypto_ai.candle`. | Must be proven in a collector-free deployment rehearsal. |
  34: | Candle processing | Bounded lanes, close-transition tokens and a local detector are implemented. | MySQL/race/crash proof remains required. |
  35: | Trading | Paper wallet and managed positions. | Live-exchange orders are outside the local-candle automatic-resolution design. |
  36: 
  37: ## Current primary flow
  38: 
  39: ```text
  40: Binance WebSocket kline
  41:   -> BinanceKlineService
  42:   -> KlineTransactionCoordinator
  43:       -> input transaction: candle + canonical 1m market price event
  44:       -> protection transaction: LivePositionProtectionService
  45:       -> observer transaction
  46:       -> close-dispatch transaction
  47:   -> CandleClosedEvent (AFTER_COMMIT)
  48:   -> CandleAnalysisDispatcher
  49:   -> CandleClosedAnalysisWorker
  50:   -> TechnicalIndicatorService
  51:   -> AnalysisService / TradeSignal
  52:   -> SignalProcessingCoordinator
  53:   -> PaperTradingService / wallet and managed-position state
  54: ```
  55: 
  56: `KlineTransactionCoordinator` preserves input -> protection -> observer ->
  57: close-dispatch ordering. Closed-candle analysis is asynchronous and must not
  58: run on the Binance ingestion thread.
  59: 
  60: ## Current core persistence
  61: 
  62: | Table/domain | Purpose |
  63: |---|---|
  64: | `candle` | Local OHLCV candle history keyed by symbol, interval, and open time. |
  65: | `technical_indicator` | Persisted indicator snapshots derived from closed candles. |
  66: | `trade_signal` | Persisted analysis decisions and their context. |
  67: | `signal_processing_work` | Durable processing registration/status for a persisted signal. |
  68: | `market_price_event` | Canonical 1m price evidence used by protection and Replay. |
  69: | Paper wallet / position tables | Paper executions, open-position state, TP/SL/profit protection. |
  70: 
  71: ## Legacy/shared-market path
  72: 
  73: **Current but pending retirement from the trader runtime.**
  74: 
  75: The shared path includes `SharedMarketSource`, `SharedMarketConsumer`,
  76: `SharedAnalysisDispatcher`, cutover/readiness logic, and local historical
  77: tables such as `shared_market_event_delivery`, `shared_market_consumer_state`,
  78: and `shared_market_health`. These records remain evidence and must not be
  79: dropped, reset, or used to manufacture an approval.
  80: 
  81: The collector event/cursor source is `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event`
  82: and `market_data_stream_cursor`. In the target local-candle runtime, the
  83: trader must not connect to, read, or write these collector tables.
  84: 
  85: `V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` is an existing Java Flyway migration
  86: that requires the collector event table for a fresh installation. A trader-only
  87: bootstrap policy is therefore **pending** and remains a release blocker. Do
  88: not edit applied Flyway migrations or Flyway history.
  89: 
  90: ## Implemented, unverified: local-candle architecture (FIX-153)
  91: 
  92: The implemented intended mode is `shared-market.mode=OFF` with trader-owned Binance kline
  93: ingestion and local candle writes. The shared executors and direct-ingestion
  94: path must never run together.
  95: 
  96: Implemented safeguards:
  97: 
  98: - Candle writes are monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or overwrite a
  99:   closed candle.
 100: - A closed-candle transition is admitted exactly once using an atomic upsert
 101:   result plus in-process admission coordination.
 102: - A bounded local detector recovers a closed candle that was committed before
 103:   the in-memory event/dispatcher could start analysis.
 104: - Fresh candles may execute; stale candles are analysed historically only, or
 105:   their already-owned work is expired without business execution.
 106: - Analysis lanes are bounded. Under overload, 1m may retain only the latest
 107:   pending candle; higher intervals use an explicit bounded FIFO policy.
 108: - Historic shared `REVIEW_REQUIRED` evidence must not block OFF-mode work.
 109: - OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must use local-candle health and must not
 110:   query retired shared-market tables.
 111: - Current REST backfill can finalise an interrupted open row but cannot mint a
 112:   runtime close token or dispatch analysis. Runtime close tokens plus
 113:   provenance keep backfill out of detector admission. The approved, pending
 114:   automatic-reconciliation extension will also insert an exact wholly absent
 115:   candle only inside the active quality window, with `BACKFILL` provenance and
 116:   the same no-token/no-dispatch boundary.
 117: - Observed local behavior (2026-10-09): a WebSocket reconnect at a candle-close
 118:   boundary can leave the already-persisted 1m/5m rows open because Binance does
 119:   not replay the final closed-kline event. The current detector recovers missing
 120:   analysis admission, not missing close facts. An authoritative, bounded REST
 121:   repair may therefore finalise the exact `closed=0` row or, under the approved
 122:   gap-backfill scope, insert an exact absent identity. It must preserve existing
 123:   provenance, use `BACKFILL` for an insertion, create no close-transition token,
 124:   and never dispatch historical work. Automatic reconciliation of this reconnect
 125:   case remains a FIX-153 release gate; the one-time local repair is evidence,
 126:   not proof that the gap is closed.
 127: - Approved pending reconciliation design: gap backfill is insert-only (no
 128:   duplicate-key update); it can never alter a live row that appears during a
 129:   REST request. Candidate selection is bounded to the active quality window;
 130:   terminal audit records are not retried. Every candle/work route must produce
 131:   a durable terminal outcome or remain explicitly retryable after a transient
 132:   failure. Historical-only processing has no execution authority.
 133: - The pending universal mutation barrier is an in-memory drain participant,
 134:   not a database or persistent trading fence. Outside drain it must not delay,
 135:   queue, or block fresh trading, signals, or protection. Open-position
 136:   protection during application replacement remains an unresolved release gate.
 137: - Local source now contains the first reconciliation implementation slice:
 138:   V107 durable audit/outcome tables, update-only authoritative open-row repair,
 139:   pure insert-only gap backfill, a dedicated recovery executor, a reconnect
 140:   request hook, and drain participation. It is disabled by default and remains
 141:   unvalidated on MySQL; it has no deployment approval.
 142: - Direct prices are normalised to `DECIMAL(30,12)` before persistence,
 143:   protection and completion. Equal timestamps have one canonical receipt.
 144: - Every processing attempt is freshness-checked after normal locks using the
 145:   database-to-exchange correction. JVM-origin timestamp decisions use the
 146:   separately measured JVM-to-exchange correction. The measurement performs no
 147:   HTTP under execution locks and uses a bounded five-second time-only client.
 148:   General Binance REST calls use the configured request timeout, and scheduled
 149:   clock sampling has a four-thread Spring scheduler pool.
 150:   Bootstrap obtains time/klines before opening its local candle-write
 151:   transaction, so a stalled REST request does not retain database resources.
 152:   REST bootstrap gets Binance server time before klines.
 153: - An ADMIN-only drain stops analysis admission, exits the JVM on successful
 154:   quiesce, and resumes admission on timeout. Price protection pauses during
 155:   the controlled drain window.
 156: 
 157: ## Implemented, unverified: protection price-event state machine
 158: 
 159: For direct 1m price observations, the target evidence lifecycle is:
 160: 
 161: ```text
 162: PENDING
 163:   -> APPLIED                 (protection effects and state commit together)
 164:   -> NON_CANONICAL           (invalid, stale, out-of-order, or duplicate)
 165:   -> PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED  (confirmed rollback/crash resolution)
 166: ```
 167: 
 168: Replay and price authority consume only accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` rows.
 169: Replay must reject a parity claim when the requested range contains unresolved
 170: direct `PENDING` evidence; this must not block live trading.
 171: 
 172: The automatic resolver is valid only for the paper-trading phase, where every
 173: protection effect commits in the same transaction as its `APPLIED` update.
 174: Before deployment, the code must prove that there are no asynchronous,
 175: `REQUIRES_NEW`, in-memory-only, or external effects in this boundary. A future
 176: live-exchange order needs a separate idempotent/exchange-reconciliation design.
 177: 
 178: The target runtime also requires exactly one active trader process. This is an
 179: operational prerequisite, not a database lease or fence.
 180: 
 181: ## Pending: FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY validation
 182: 
 183: FIX-154 is separate from the local-candle change and is not implemented.
 184: 
 185: Its purpose is to let a historical bearish 1h signal affect a current 1m BUY
 186: only when the historical evidence is fresh, quality-valid, and sufficiently
 187: strong. `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` on a historical signal is investigatory
 188: evidence, not a higher-timeframe quality gate; the current 1m BTC protection
 189: remains unchanged.
 190: 
 191: The design includes reliability bands (`NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`,
 192: `HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE`), required fresh/quality-valid bearish 5m support for
 193: a hard veto, persisted `trade_signal.data_quality_valid`, recovery release
 194: rules, immutable outcome audit evidence, and Replay/no-look-ahead parity.
 195: 
 196: The implementation design is maintained in `codex-docs/FIX-154_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md`.
 197: It supersedes no existing deployed behavior and is deliberately separate from
 198: the unrelated legacy readiness document under `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md`.
 199: 
 200: ## Deployment posture
 201: 
 202: Neither FIX-153 nor FIX-154 is deployment-ready. FIX-153 source hardening is
 203: implemented but unverified; FIX-154 remains pending.
 204: 
 205: Required validation includes Java 21 build/tests, disposable MySQL bootstrap,
 206: real-MySQL concurrency and lock-wait tests, crash/restart tests, stale-input
 207: tests, Replay parity checks, OFF-mode health/admin checks, collector-free
 208: runtime verification, deterministic dispatcher/detector/drain tests,
 209: two-process Windows lock rehearsal, and sustained-load observation across
 210: enabled symbols and intervals. This local workspace has Java 17 and cannot
 211: compile the Java 21 project.
 212: 
 213: No production configuration, database, service, or deployment change is made
 214: without explicit user approval.
 215: 
 216: ## FIX-153 validation status (2026-10-09)
 217: 
 218: The reconciliation component now has deterministic Java 21 coverage for its
 219: core admission and outcome contract. The tests execute the real audit and
 220: outcome stores against H2 while mocking only Binance and the candle write
 221: boundary. They prove that healthy delayed live closes are not taken over and
 222: that recovery remains token-free/non-live when it is positively evidenced.
 223: 
 224: The real-MySQL suite contains matching update-only and insert-only assertions,
 225: but it requires a deliberately disposable loopback MySQL instance and is not
 226: run by the ordinary unit-test goal. This is a release gate, not a warning that
 227: can be waived. The shared Binance 429/418 admission gate, universal mutation
 228: barrier, structural historical-only isolation, financial-effect reconciler,
 229: atomicity/crash proofs, replacement-time position protection, Replay parity,
 230: and soak validation remain incomplete. Therefore `local-candle.reconciliation.enabled`
 231: must stay `false`.

 233: The single `local-candle` configuration mapping is a startup invariant. It now
 234: contains both reconciliation and existing local-runtime properties; a second

   1: # Local candle coverage check Г?" 2026-10-09
   2: 
   3: - Scope: read-only check of all 21 enabled symbols in `crypto_ai.candle`, over the two-hour window ending at the database UTC clock.
   4: - Current state: all 63 enabled symbol/interval lanes (1m, 5m, 1h) have closed-candle records and current live ingestion. The newest 1m candle was 54 seconds old at the check.
   5: - Continuity: 5m and 1h had no internal gaps in the inspected window. Twelve symbols had one missing 1m candle: the interval from `2026-10-08T22:18:00Z` to `2026-10-08T22:19:00Z` (KSA `01:18` to `01:19`). This coincides with the local application startup period; the check establishes the gap but does not prove its cause.
   6: - Impact: current live signals are being generated, but the missing historical 1m candles should be reconciled before relying on uninterrupted historical indicator lineage for that startup window. No database data or code was changed by this check.
   7: 
   8: # Signal-staleness root cause Г?" 2026-10-09
   9: 
  10: - System Health CRITICAL signal-staleness alerts were confirmed as accurate for 1m and 5m analysis, not a dashboard timezone issue. At the check, candles continued through `2026-10-09T06:43:00Z` (1m) and `06:35:00Z` (5m), while the newest live signals were `04:34:03Z` (1m) and `04:30:07Z` (5m).
  11: - The most recent 210-candle data-quality window has a synchronized gap for every enabled symbol: missing 1m open `2026-10-09T04:34:00Z` (KSA 07:34) and missing 5m open `2026-10-09T04:30:00Z` (KSA 07:30).
  12: - Cause confirmed from the WebSocket lifecycle log: generation 1 was revoked at `2026-10-09T04:35:00.133951100Z`; generation 2 was admitted at `04:35:01.369103400Z` (1,236 ms interruption). The affected 1m and 5m candles had both closed at the reconnect boundary. Binance did not replay their final closed-kline events, leaving the already-created local rows open rather than finalized.
  13: - `CandleDataQualityService` is configured with `minimum-candles: 210` and `maximum-missing-candles: 0`. Therefore one missing candle makes the entire relevant analysis window invalid. Runtime logs confirm `DATA_QUALITY_BLOCKED` with `Missing 1 candle(s) in the analysis window` for current 1m and 5m worker attempts.
  14: - 1h has no comparable gap and produced a live signal at `2026-10-09T06:00:03Z` (KSA 09:00).
  15: - No repair or configuration change was made. Correct remediation must restore authoritative missing candles rather than relaxing the zero-missing data-quality safety rule.
  16: 
  17: ## Bounded local repair completed
  18: 
  19: - Authorized local repair finalized the 42 interrupted `RUNTIME` rows using Binance REST candles for the exact identities above: 21 x 1m and 21 x 5m.
  20: - Each response was validated for the expected open and close timestamp before one transaction finalized only a row where `closed=0`. Existing closed candles, provenance, and close-transition tokens were not changed; no close-dispatch, analysis, signal, or trade was initiated by the repair.
  21: - Verification: all 1m/5m 210-candle quality windows had zero gaps after the repair. The next live close produced new LIVE signals at `2026-10-09T07:30:07Z` for both 1m and 5m.
  22: 
  23: # ETHUSDT paper-trade 59 investigation Г?" 2026-10-09
  24: 
  25: - Scope: local `crypto_ai` paper trade `wallet_trade.id=59` for `ETHUSDT`; all database reads were bounded and read-only. Stored UTC timestamps are shown below with their KSA (`UTC+03:00`) interpretation.
  26: - Entry: signal `trade_signal.id=69226` was generated at `2026-10-09T07:33:00.233691Z` (10:33:00.234 KSA) from the 1m candle opened at 10:32 KSA. It was a `BUY`, score 79, confidence 70, entry allowed, at 2502.77. The trade executed at `07:33:01.654548Z` (10:33:01.655 KSA), 1.42 seconds after the signal; this is not an execution-latency issue.
  27: - Exit: `wallet_trade.id=60` closed the position at 2504.30 at `07:34:34.196100Z` (10:34:34.196 KSA) with `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. The managed position recorded a high of 2505.80 and an activated profit-lock price of 2504.429502243252. The realized paper gain was about 0.02445 USDT before fees (0.015982291620 ETH x 1.53 USDT).
  28: - Market path: ETH rose from 2485.88 at 07:54 KSA to 2502.77 at entry (+16.89, +0.68%). It reached 2505.56 at 10:25 KSA before the entry; entry was 2.79 (0.11%) below that high. The post-entry high was 2505.93 at 10:34 KSA, only 3.16 (0.13%) above entry, then the candle retraced. This supports the user's observation that entry occurred late in the observed move.
