  45: ### Q1. Why did it exit on profit lock instead of take-profit? [PE]
  46: - **TP needed three ticks.** TP was 0.00000543373: entry 0.00000541 plus 3 A- the 1m ATR of 0.00000000791. Prices move in steps of 0.00000001, so reaching TP required a price of 0.00000544.
  47: - **The lock armed at two ticks.**
  48:   - 0.00000543 was 84.2815% of the way to TP, past the 70% activation level.
  49:   - That counts as one completed 10% step, so the lock was set at 50% of the TP distance: 0.000005421865, about 1.19 ticks above entry.
  50:   - The settings are confirmed twice: the runtime log shows `activationPct=70.0000`, and trade 107's stored message shows activation 70%, initial lock 40%, trail 10%.
  51: - **One tick down triggered it.** 0.00000542 was at or below the lock and above the hard floor of 0.000005412705, so the exit policy ran.
  52: - **What the exit policy saw:**
  53: 
  54:   | Timeframe | Signal | Label | Age at the trigger |
  55:   |---|---|---|---|
  56:   | 1m | 89795 | WATCH | 41.8 s |
  57:   | 5m | 89724 | WATCH | 3 m 38.8 s |
  58:   | 1h | 88327 | NEUTRAL | 58 m 37.1 s |
  59: 
  60:   The labels are stored in trade 107's message. The signal IDs are my inference from the signal times in DBQ-010 [AI, high confidence].
  61: - **Neither hold rule matched.**
  62:   - A hold needs either a 1h BUY/STRONG_BUY with a supportive 5m, or a 1h WATCH with a supportive 5m and a 1m that is not bearish.
  63:   - A NEUTRAL 1h fails both.
  64:   - The 1h was not bearish, and the 1m and 5m were not both bearish, so the rule fell through to the default `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`.
  65: - **No accepted price reached TP.** The corrected evidence chain is in objection S1.
  66: - **Classification:** a valid exit that followed the implemented rules. The decision came 94 ms after the price was observed. It was not stale data, an execution delay, a configuration problem or a logic defect.
  67: 
  68: ### Q2. Why was there no continuation into 10 October? [PE]
  69: - In production, the target-extension check (`PositionContinuationPolicy`) is called from only one place: the live TP branch. Every other caller is a Replay class. This position never entered the TP branch.
  70: - The position closed at 23:58:43 KSA, 39 min 16 s before the 00:38 KSA candle. Nothing was open to extend.
  71: - "12:38" means 00:38 KSA [AI from PE]. The query log records the user's earlier times on a 12-hour clock ("11:44", "12:15"), and the investigation run began at 01:09 KSA on 10 October.
  72: - *Outcome only:* the market first traded at TP in the 00:04 KSA candle, about 5Г?"6 minutes after the exit. Whether an extension would have passed then depends on 1m trend and momentum scores that aren't in the evidence. The exit could not have known any of this.
  73: 
  74: ### Q3. Why didn't protection re-check the remaining upside before selling? [PE unless marked]
  75: - **The lock-breach rule reads three labels and nothing else.** It does not look at distance to TP, momentum, the order book or the advisory analysis.
  76: - **A hold was impossible for the whole trade [AI from PE].**
  77:   - The deciding input was the 1h label.
  78:   - It was NEUTRAL from 20:00:06Z, before entry, and the next 1h signal (21:00:12Z) came after the exit and was also NEUTRAL.
  79:   - The entry signal 89469 had already recorded "1h=NEUTRAL".
  80:   - So any lock breach during this hold had to sell.
  81: - **Entry and hold use different 1h standards [AI from PE].**
  82:   - The entry was allowed under the BALANCED_STRONG profile, which accepts a bullish 5m with a NEUTRAL 1h.
  83:   - A hold after a lock breach requires the 1h to be WATCH or better.
  84:   - About 20 s after the exit, the same profile refused a new entry for signal 89815 (5m WATCH, 1h NEUTRAL).
  85: - **The price grid compressed the protection layers [AI from PE].**
  86:   - TP was only 2.373 ticks above entry.
  87:   - The lock armed at +2 ticks and fired at +1.
  88:   - Near-TP protection arms at 90% of the TP distance. On this grid that is the same +3 tick price as TP, and the TP branch runs first, so Near-TP protection could never arm (stored state: INACTIVE).
  89: - **The advisory analysis said HOLD 15 times during the trade** (exit scores 0Г?"7 out of 25). Live protection never reads it, and the advisory service can only execute a stop-loss.
  90: - **The signal path would most likely have sold anyway [AI from PE].** Its profit-lock exit reads no labels. The next 1m signal, 89815 at 0.00000542, would most likely have closed the position at the same price about 20 s later.
  91: - **The protection levels ignore fees [AI from PE + ER].**
  92:   - Paper trades record a fee of 0.
  93:   - At 0.1% per side, the +0.046211 USDT gross gain becomes about Г^'0.0038 USDT net.
  94:   - The lock's "protected" level (+0.22%) and the +0.05% hard floor are close to or below a 0.2% round-trip fee.
  95: - The later price rise is outcome only.

 139: **[ER] External research**
 140: - [Binance Developer Docs, "WebSocket Streams" (Spot)](https://developers.binance.com/docs/binance-spot-api-docs/web-socket-streams). No publication date shown; fetched this round.
 141:   - Finding: the kline stream "push[es] updates to the current klines/candlestick every second", with an update speed of "1000ms for 1s, 2000ms for the other intervals". Each update carries both the close `c` and the high `h`.
 142:   - Limitation: the page doesn't say whether a push is skipped when nothing changed. During the hold, SHIB updates arrived 2 s to 59 s apart, which suggests pushes only on change [AI].
 143:   - Relevance: protection sees the close at most once every 2 s. A price spike between updates shows up only in `h`, which protection does not use.
 144: - [Binance Fee Schedule](https://www.binance.com/en/fee/schedule) and [exchangeInfo SHIBUSDT](https://api.binance.com/api/v3/exchangeInfo?symbol=SHIBUSDT), carried over from round 1 and not re-fetched.
 145:   - Findings: 0.100% regular spot fee per side; SHIB price step 0.00000001.

 148: ## OBJECTIONS
 149: **To my own round 4**
 150: - **S1. A step in my proof was wrong (the conclusion is not).**
 151:   - I argued that because the profit-lock evaluator logged nothing between 20:47:41.566Z and 20:58:09.534Z, no accepted price went above 0.00000542.
 152:   - That only rules out 0.00000543. A price at or above TP enters the TP branch and returns at `LivePositionProtectionService.java:170`, before the lock evaluator runs at `:185`, so it would never produce a lock log.
 153:   - "No accepted price reached TP" rests instead on five points:
 154:     - (a) The whole archive has zero lines matching `(TAKE_PROFIT|TP_EXTENSION).*SHIBUSDT`. Both possible TP outcomes, extending the target or selling, write log lines that include the symbol (`:120, :164, :168`).
 155:     - (b) The TP stored on managed position 64 never changed.
 156:     - (c) There is no TAKE_PROFIT trade; trade IDs 106 and 107 are consecutive.
 157:     - (d) A silent failed sell is ruled out: position quantity is checked at `:93` in the same transaction, and SELL 107 sold the full quantity with no trade in between.
 158:     - (e) The protection-failure and pending-resolved counters stayed at 0.
 159: - **S2. One claim was right but poorly supported.** I wrote "protection only ever accepted 0.00000541Г+'0.00000542Г+'0.00000543Г+'0.00000542", but before the lock armed, the logs only record new highs, not every price. It is still true, now supported by:
 160:   - the lowest 1m low from 20:45Z being 0.00000541 (DBQ-008);
 161:   - the new-high logs, which never exceed 0.00000543;
 162:   - point S1, which rules out anything at or above TP.
 163: - **S3. "The three patterns cover every rejection path" was overstated.** They cover every rejection inside price-event handling, but not messages dropped earlier in the pipeline:
 164:   - a WebSocket connection-gate rejection (`MESSAGE_REJECTED`, which doesn't name the symbol);
 165:   - a failed callback (`CALLBACK_FAILED`);
 166:   - a drain during shutdown (`KLINE_REJECTED_DRAINING`).
 167: 
 168:   Neither agent searched for these. Gate rejections are ruled out because the gate only ever moves forward (pending Г+' admitting Г+' revoked) and was still admitting SHIB messages at 20:58:00 and 20:58:43. Drain only happens at shutdown, and the counters show no restart. Failed-callback lines remain unsearched (Unknown 4).
 169: - **S4. "105 ms to the SELL" needs qualifying.** That is the timestamp set inside the transaction. The exit was logged and committed at 20:58:43.837/.839Z, 390 ms after the price was observed. Still no material delay.
 170:

 172: - **C1.** "Below TP Г?▌ but below the lock" should say "and below the lock". 20:58:09.444Z is when the price was observed; the lock was saved at 20:58:09.534Z.
 173: - **C2.** Codex's "silent failed sell" objection is ruled out by S1(d). The only residual is an unrecorded manual wallet edit, with no evidence of one.
 174: - **C3.** The "if 12:38 meant noon" hedge is resolved (see Q2).
 175: - **C4.** Codex's round-5 point, that the policy doesn't check whether the labels are fresh, is true of the code (`:95-97`). In this trade, though, all three labels were the newest available for their timeframes. Any breach late in an hour will see a 1h label close to an hour old. This describes the implemented rule, not a defect in this trade.
 176: - **C5.** Codex missed the same two gaps I corrected in S1 and S3. Its conclusions are unaffected.
 177: 
 178: **Process**
 179: - Query IDs DBQ-004, 005 and 006 were reused and their first results overwritten (`DATABASE_QUERY_LOG.md:62-117` vs `:271-326`).
 180: - The result files display KSA times, although the log policy says raw values are UTC. For example, DBQ-014's 23:58:09.534 is the runtime log's 20:58:09.534Z.
 181: - DBQ-008's columns `first_open_utc` and `last_open_utc` are mislabelled; they hold KSA times.
 182: - No conclusion depends on any of this.
 183: 
 184: **Statistics and Replay**
 185: - One trade whose price later rose proves nothing about a better rule. Picking it because of what happened afterwards is outcome-selection bias. Any rule change would need multi-symbol, out-of-sample testing that accounts for fees, slippage and price order within each candle.
 186: - Exact-price Replay follows the live order and would read the same price events, but it skips the signal-path exits.
 187: - Fallback Replay applies target extension and lock holds to signal prices, which production's signal path does not do.
 188: - Replay was not run, so I make no parity claim.
 189: 
 190: **Entry route (outside Q1Г?"Q3; not blocking)**
 191: - **E1.** The wake-up route bought 1.1 s after 89469's own evaluation said "Wait for fresh evidence to rebuild".
 192:   - The route accepts opportunities still marked BUILDING and does not re-check that blocker.
 193:   - Its message "after the prior blocker cleared" is fixed text (`ExecutionIntelligenceService.java:446`), not a condition the code verifies.
 194:   - This is within the design, since the fresh 5m BUY counts as the new evidence, but the stored explanation overstates what was checked.
 195: - **E2.** Risk/reward was 0.877: stop 0.5% below entry, TP 0.44% above. The wake-up route has no minimum risk/reward check; other entry routes require 1.0Г?"1.25. This is a design observation, not a rule violation.
 196: 
 197: ## UNKNOWNS
 198: 1. **Did Binance trade at 0.00000544 or above between 20:45Z and 20:54Z?**
 199:    - The 1m candle highs for those minutes aren't in the evidence.
 200:    - One bounded read-only query of `candle` (SHIBUSDT, 1m, 20:45Г?"20:54Z, `high_price`) would settle it.
 201:    - The answer can't change rule compliance. It could change the story from "TP was never reached" to "TP was touched between 2-second updates".
 202:    - [AI] That is unlikely: it would need a jump of two or more ticks and a retreat within one update window, with no 0.00000543 update in between.
 203: 2. **Which code was actually running is unproven.**
 204:    - The working tree has uncommitted changes to the live protection, price-event, kline-ingestion and wallet-execution classes.
 205:    - Corroboration: trade 107's stored text matches `PositionExitPolicy.java:88-92` and `DynamicProfitLockService.java:179-182, 193-195` word for word.
 206: 3. **Replay was not run.** Running it creates Replay state and needs the user's consent.

===== agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION.md =====
   1: # Replay / Production Business-Logic-Parity Redesign
   2: 
   3: **Revision:** RPR-0.4-DRAFT  
   4: **Status:** DRAFT ƒ?" NOT APPROVED ƒ?" IMPLEMENTATION FORBIDDEN  
   5: **Created:** 2026-10-10  
   6: **Scope owner:** Codex implementation / Claude independent review
   7: 
   8: ## 1. Business objective
   9: 
  10: Make manual Replay reproduce the current paper-trading Production business flow
  11: as closely as durable historical evidence permits, while remaining completely
  12: isolated from Production trading state and wallet data.
  13: 
  14: The exact intended flow is:
  15: 
  16: ```text
  17: historical candle + historical context + canonical accepted price events
  18:   -> shared Production indicator/signal/decision policies
  19:   -> replay-local virtual wallet/account state
  20:   -> shared sizing/allocation/entry revalidation policies
  21:   -> replay-local position lifecycle
  22:   -> shared TP/SL/profit-lock/continuation/exit policies
  23:   -> replay output + explicit parity certificate
  24: ```
  25: 
  26: Replay must never write `wallet_*`, `paper_position`, `trade_signal`,
  27: `signal_processing_work`, or any other Production trading table.
  28: 
  29: The result is not a claim that historical Java scheduling, MySQL lock timing,
  30: network delay, external Binance availability, or the historical Production
  31: wallet balance can be reproduced. It is a verified **business-logic parity**
  32: claim only when all required market/decision evidence exists. Otherwise Replay
  33: remains available as a labelled simulation and may not claim parity.
  34: 
  35: ## 2. Confirmed root cause and evidence
  36: 
  37: The 2026-10-10 source audit established the following:
  38: 
  39: 1. Signal, entry, normal-exit, continuation, profit-lock, and near-TP policy
  40:    use several shared Production services.
  41: 2. `ShadowProductionReplayService` separately owns replay wallet cash,
  42:    positions, daily budget, persistence, price-loop lifecycle, and result
  43:    labels. It starts at `INITIAL_CAPITAL=10000`, runs one symbol/position, and
  44:    does not reproduce Production wallet/global controls.
  45: 3. A run is labelled `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` when at least one accepted price
  46:    event exists, rather than proving coverage for every interval while a
  47:    position was open.
  48: 4. Historical derivatives information is not persisted;
  49:    `DerivativesPositioningService.evaluateHistorical()` returns UNAVAILABLE.
  50:    Historical order-book evaluation can likewise be unavailable when snapshots
  51:    were not retained.
  52: 5. Replay-only FIX-11K and FIX-11Q observers, and the pre-FIX-122 UI revision,
  53:    have no current Production equivalent and must not contaminate parity runs.
  54: 6. Current Replay reads `WalletSettingsRepository` directly and its near-TP
  55:    path can call `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy`, which can request a current
  56:    live Binance minimum notional. Both violate replay account/external-input
  57:    isolation.
  58: 
  59: ## 3. Boundaries
  60: 
  61: ### In scope
  62: 
  63: - Replay input/evidence qualification and run-level parity status.
  64: - Replay-local virtual wallet state, wallet controls, positions, executions,
  65:   daily statistics, and global limit reproduction.
  66: - Canonical price-event coverage qualification and event ordering.
  67: - Historical context-evidence qualification for derivatives and order book.
  68: - Removal of approved retired Replay-only business/experiment behaviour.
  69: - Parity certificate, diagnostics, migrations, tests, documentation.
  70: 
  71: ### Out of scope
  72: 
  73: - Any Production strategy change, sizing change, exit change, or wallet change.
  74: - Live-exchange orders, fills, fees, slippage, or order-book execution modelling.
  75: - Replaying database locks, thread scheduling, JVM pauses, WebSocket transport,
  76:   external REST failures, or transaction retries as historical facts.
  77: - FIX-153, FIX-154, and FIX-155 trading-policy changes.
  78: - Deleting Production evidence or existing historical Replay/archive rows.
  79: 
  80: ## 4. Contractual modes
  81: 
  82: | Mode | Purpose | Missing evidence behaviour | Claim allowed |
  83: |---|---|---|---|
  84: | `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` | Reproduce Production rules using an explicitly selected virtual Replay account and complete market/decision evidence. | Refuse the run before execution. | Business-logic parity. |
  85: | `SIMULATION` | Manual what-if/research run. | Continue but persist all approximation reasons. | Simulation only. |
  86: | `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` | Explicit, separately approved research such as FIX-155. | Per experiment contract. | Never Production parity. |
  87: 
  88: The default Administration Replay action must be `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY`; the UI
  89: must make `SIMULATION` and `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` explicit opt-in choices.
  90: 
  91: ## 5. Business-logic-parity eligibility
  92: 
  93: A `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` run must fail before generated signals or shadow wallet
  94: state are written unless all of the following hold:
  95: 
  96: 1. Every requested candle identity is closed and within the quality contract.
  97: 2. A complete immutable `ReplayAccountProfile` is supplied: starting virtual
  98:    USDT, reserve, daily budget, daily loss state, maximum-new-position/global
  99:    limits, and wallet settings. It is copied to replay-only tables at run start
 100:    and is never read from or written to a Production wallet table.
 101: 3. The selected replay universe is declared. The virtual account applies
 102:    global limits across every symbol included in that run; no claim is made
 103:    that it reproduces the historical Production wallet's other positions.
 104: 4. The selected profile contains versioned execution constraints for relevant
 105:    symbols, including minimum executable notional. Replay never fetches a
 106:    current exchange constraint while evaluating historical events.
 107: 5. Every canonical direct price observation required from the first replay
 108:    position open through its terminal close is represented by accepted
 109:    `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` evidence, with no unresolved `PENDING` row in the whole
 110:    consumed range.
 111: 6. Required historical order-book evidence exists whenever the feature was
 112:    enabled for the corresponding Production decision.
 113: 7. Required historical derivatives evidence exists whenever derivatives were
 114:    enabled and could change confidence or entry authority. Until durable
 115:    derivatives persistence exists, affected requests must be refused in this
 116:    mode rather than silently treating UNAVAILABLE as equivalent.
 117: 8. The exact Production configuration/version fingerprint is available for the
 118:    requested period, or all version-dependent decision paths are marked
 119:    unavailable and the mode is refused.
 120: 
 121: `SIMULATION` may use configured defaults or fixed starting capital but must
 122: store each deviation in its output. It may never be displayed as parity.
 123: 
 124: ### 5.1 Wallet isolation boundary
 125: 
 126: Replay never reads Production `wallet_asset`, `wallet_settings`,
 127: `wallet_trade`, `wallet_managed_position`, `paper_position`, daily-statistics,
 128: or wallet-snapshot rows as a decision input. The existing data does not retain
 129: complete historical wallet state and is deliberately irrelevant to a Replay
 130: account. A user-selected/versioned `ReplayAccountProfile` is the only account
 131: input. This preserves safe isolation while exercising the same pure sizing and
 132: position-management formulas.
 133: 
 134: The profile also contains symbol execution constraints used by shared
 135: paper-order guards. `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy` must be split into a pure
 136: minimum-notional evaluator plus a Production-only exchange-constraint provider;
 137: Replay receives immutable profile values and may not issue Binance HTTP.
 138: 
 139: ## 6. Agreed behaviour changes
 140: 
 141: ### 6.1 Replay starting state
 142: 
 143: Replace hard-coded starting capital with an immutable `ReplayStartingState`.
 144: It is seeded from the selected, versioned `ReplayAccountProfile` and stored
 145: under the replay run ID before processing begins. Shadow wallet/position
 146: services consume only this replay-local state and replay tables.
 147: 
 148: The run-start state must include profile ID/version, creation timestamp, version/config fingerprint,
 149: virtual USDT/asset balances, daily-budget state, daily loss state, virtual
 150: managed positions, virtual paper positions, replay idempotency keys, and a
 151: versioned wallet/settings/configuration fingerprint. It may not be refreshed
 152: from Production while a run is executing.
 153: 
 154: ### 6.2 Shared paper-wallet business authority
 155: 
 156: Extract or complete pure shared policies for:
 157: 
 158: - initial and progressive sizing;
 159: - reserve, daily budget, daily-loss, maximum-new-position, and global
 160:   maximum-open-position controls;
 161: - buy/sell/partial-harvest quantity and cost-basis calculations;
 162: - position average entry, target/stop updates, profit-lock state, and near-TP
 163:   state transitions;
 164: - idempotency-key interpretation.
 165: 
 166: Production retains its current transactional repositories. Replay receives
 167: replay-local adapters implementing the same policy inputs/outputs. No policy
 168: may branch on `replay=true` to produce a different business decision.
 169: 
 170: ### 6.3 Price evidence
 171: 
 172: Replace the boolean/label derived from ƒ?oany event existsƒ?? with a coverage
 173: result containing requested interval, covered interval, gaps, accepted event
 174: count, rejected/unresolved count, and every position-open interval requiring
 175: protection.
 176: 
 177: `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` rejects incomplete coverage. `SIMULATION` may use
 178: signal-price fallback only with the persisted reason `PRICE_PATH_INCOMPLETE`.
 179: 
 180: ### 6.4 Historical decision evidence
 181: 
 182: Persist versioned derivatives and order-book observations required by
 183: Production, or mark affected historical intervals ineligible for business-logic
 184: parity. Replay must use the same normalized evaluator and only evidence at or
 185: before the replay clock. It must never call live Binance endpoints.
 186: 
 187: ### 6.5 Event ordering and time
 188: 
 189: Use canonical UTC `observed_at` and event IDs for price ordering. Define a
 190: single deterministic tie order matching Productionƒ?Ts committed sequence:
 191: 
 192: 1. accepted price protection event;
 193: 2. resulting position/wallet state;
 194: 3. closed-candle analysis signal;
 195: 4. signal-driven position action.
 196: 
 197: If durable evidence cannot establish the required tie order, business-logic parity
 198: must refuse the run. Replay timestamps remain presentation-neutral UTC;
 199: KSA conversion belongs only to UI/reporting.
 200: 
 201: ### 6.6 Retired Replay-only behaviour
 202: 
 203: The following are **candidates for removal**, subject to complete-reference
 204: review and approval. They must be removed from baseline parity runs now; any
 205: future research requires an independently approved module and run mode.
 206: 
 207: | Registry ID | Candidate | Proposed disposition |
 208: |---|---|---|
 209: | RPR-RM-01 | `DefensiveRiskReductionReplayObserver` / FIX-11K | Remove source, controller/UI/output tables only after archive/export compatibility is proved. |
 210: | RPR-RM-02 | `OneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver` / FIX-11Q | Remove source, controller/UI/output tables only after archive/export compatibility is proved. |
 211: | RPR-RM-03 | pre-FIX-122 replay revision selector | Remove the UI/API option and old comparison path; Production-current FIX-122 remains the only parity rule. |
 212: | RPR-RM-04 | `EXPERIMENTAL` logic mode if no approved user-facing experiment consumes it | Remove after exact reference search; otherwise retain as an isolated framework with no baseline access. |
 213: 
 214: `ReplayDataSource.DATABASE` versus `DATASET` is not retired business logic:
 215: both must remain only if a test proves equal outputs. It is a data-access
 216: implementation choice, not an old Production-rule selector.
 217: 
 218: ## 7. Implementation change registry
 219: 
 220: No implementation item is authorized until this exact revision is approved.
 221: 
 222: | ID | Area | Required change |
 223: |---|---|---|
 224: | RPR-01 | run contract | Add persisted mode, evidence status, account profile/state ID, version/config fingerprint, and refusal reason to the replay run. |
 225: | RPR-02 | replay account schema/service | Add `ReplayAccountProfile` and immutable run-start-state tables. Seed virtual state only from the selected Replay profile. |
 226: | RPR-03 | wallet authority | Extract/reuse pure paper-wallet policy and provide Production/replay persistence adapters; remove direct Replay reads of `WalletSettingsRepository`. |
 227: | RPR-03a | execution constraints | Split minimum-notional evaluation from live exchange constraint acquisition; inject immutable profile constraints into Replay and prohibit Replay Binance HTTP. |
 228: | RPR-04 | shadow replay | Replace fixed capital/local single-position assumptions with replay-local state and multi-symbol/global-limit coordinator. |
 229: | RPR-05 | price evidence | Add interval coverage validator and replace the current any-event exact-price label. |
 230: | RPR-06 | historical context | Add versioned persistence/lookup/qualification for order book and derivatives, or explicit parity refusal. |
 231: | RPR-07 | timing/order | Persist/use deterministic committed event ordering and reject unknown ordering for parity runs. |
 232: | RPR-08 | certificate | Add immutable parity certificate containing field comparisons, evidence counts, deviations, and final claim. |
 233: | RPR-09 | removals | Execute only the approved RPR-RM entries, including migration/archive compatibility. |
 234: | RPR-10 | UI/API | Make mode explicit; default to business-logic parity; display refusal/deviation/certificate details. |
 235: | RPR-11 | tests | Add unit, integration, MySQL, complete scenario, no-look-ahead, and Production/Replay comparison tests below. |
 236: | RPR-12 | docs | Update architecture, rules, changelog, investigation, operational runbook, and parity contract. |
 237: 
 238: ## 8. Error handling, concurrency, and transactions
 239: 
 240: - Replay-state creation is isolated and must be consistent for one profile version.
 241: - Business-logic-parity evidence validation happens before replay execution; no
 242:   partial shadow run may be labelled a parity run.
 243: - Replay output writes are transactionally isolated by `test_run_id`; a failed
 244:   run records its failure and certificate status without changing Production.
 245: - Replay must not lock Production wallet/position rows for business mutation.
 246: - Replay-profile/state reads must not expose credentials and must log only IDs,
 247:   UTC times, symbol counts, mode, and evidence result.
 248: - Cancellation preserves isolated run evidence and cannot leave a completed
 249:   parity certificate.
 250: 
 251: ## 9. Logging and observability registry
 252: 
 253: | ID | Event | Mandatory fields |
 254: |---|---|---|
 255: | RPR-L-01 | virtual starting state created/refused | runId, mode, profile ID/version, virtual balances/position counts, config fingerprint, reason |
 256: | RPR-L-02 | evidence qualified/refused | runId, symbol(s), from/to UTC, candle/price/orderbook/derivatives status, gap counts |
 257: | RPR-L-03 | shadow execution decision | runId, symbol, signal/candle ID, decision code, shared-policy source, replay state ID |
 258: | RPR-L-04 | parity mismatch | runId, field, Production value, replay value, evidence reference, severity |
 259: | RPR-L-05 | certificate completed | runId, claim, matched/mismatched field counts, simulation deviations, duration |
 260: | RPR-L-06 | retired path blocked | runId, removed/experimental feature ID, requested source, outcome |
 261: 
 262: ## 10. Database/configuration/trading impact
 263: 
 264: - **Database:** additive replay account/snapshot/certificate/evidence schema and any
 265:   required durable historical derivatives observations. Applied migrations are
 266:   never edited. Archive migration/retention is mandatory before deletion of
 267:   retired Replay-only output tables.
 268: - **Configuration:** add replay mode/evidence policy only. Production defaults
 269:   and trading configuration remain unchanged.
 270: - **Trading behaviour:** NONE. Production is read-only to this feature and no
 271:   live/paper Production decision, wallet mutation, position, or protection
 272:   rule may change.
 273: - **Replay behaviour:** baseline becomes evidence-qualified business-logic parity
 274:   or explicitly labelled simulation; retired experimental paths are removed or
 275:   isolated only as approved above.
 276: 
 277: ## 11. Test and validation registry
 278: 
 279: | ID | Required proof |
 280: |---|---|
 281: | RPR-T-01 | The same profile inputs produce equal Production/replay sizing, reserve, daily budget, daily-loss, and global-position-limit decisions through shared policies. |
 282: | RPR-T-01a | Replay refuses `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` when no immutable replay account profile is selected; it must never silently use a Production wallet row. |
 283: | RPR-T-02 | A missing, unresolved, rejected, or coverage-gapped price event refuses business-logic parity and labels simulation correctly. |
 284: | RPR-T-03 | Price-event/signal same-time ordering matches Production for TP, SL, profit lock, normal exit, and signal SELL. |
 285: | RPR-T-04 | End-to-end initial BUY, progressive add, target extension, rebase, stop, profit lock, near-TP partial harvest, terminal exit, and P&L lifecycle match field-for-field. |
 286: | RPR-T-05 | Historical order book/derivatives use only as-of persisted data; missing evidence refuses parity; no live HTTP occurs. |
 287: | RPR-T-05a | Replay never reads a Production wallet settings row or calls a Binance minimum-notional endpoint; profile values drive the same pure minimum-order decision. |
 288: | RPR-T-06 | Multi-symbol global wallet/max-position test proves no isolated-symbol bypass. |
 289: | RPR-T-07 | Cancellation/error tests prove Production tables remain unchanged and certificates cannot falsely pass. |
 290: | RPR-T-08 | MySQL migration and transaction tests use a disposable database. |
 291: | RPR-T-09 | Dataset and database replay inputs produce equivalent output for the same qualified evidence, or one path is removed. |
 292: | RPR-T-10 | Removal tests prove baseline Replay cannot invoke FIX-11K, FIX-11Q, or pre-FIX-122 paths. |
 293: | RPR-T-11 | Real historical parity suite compares selected known Production windows and produces stored certificates. |
 294: 
 295: ## 12. Risks, rollback, and acceptance
 296: 
 297: - Risk: historical data required for verified parity may not exist. Mitigation:
 298:   refuse parity and use clearly labelled simulation; do not invent evidence.
 299: - Risk: snapshot schema increases storage. Mitigation: bounded retention and
 300:   archive strategy, never deletion of active/investigation evidence.
 301: - Risk: retiring observers breaks historical UI/archive reads. Mitigation:
 302:   migration and archive/export compatibility tests before removal.
 303: - Rollback: disable new business-logic-parity mode and retain existing archived
 304:   output; Production requires no rollback because it is never mutated.
 305: 
 306: Acceptance requires: all registry IDs reconciled; no unregistered code change;
 307: all test evidence green; disposable MySQL validation; complete source review by
 308: Claude; Codex verification of the exact reviewed revision; and explicit user
 309: authorization after both agents approve this same document.

===== agent-docs/RPR-0.4_REVIEW_RESPONSE.md =====
   1: # RPR-0.4 independent-review response
   2: 
   3: **Date:** 2026-10-10  
   4: **Status:** Analysis response only ƒ?" RPR-0.4 remains rejected ƒ?" no implementation authorised  
   5: **Inputs reviewed:** Coordinator run `20261010-095027`, Claude rounds 1ƒ?"3 and Codex cross-examination.
   6: 
   7: ## 1. Conclusion
   8: 
   9: The independent review correctly found that RPR-0.4 is a useful direction but
  10: is not a complete implementation contract. It must not be implemented as
  11: written. This response does not amend or approve RPR-0.4; it records the
  12: required shape of a replacement revision (RPR-0.5 or later).
  13: 
  14: ## 2. Accepted material findings and required correction
  15: 
  16: | ID | Accepted finding | Required correction in the next complete specification |
  17: |---|---|---|
  18: | CR-01 | The change registry is too high-level to reconcile implementation. | Supply a registry with one ID per artifact: exact file/class/method, before/after behaviour, configuration key, database migration/table/column/index, log event/level/context, tests, documentation, rollback and Production/Replay effect. |
  19: | CR-02 | The proposal changes shared Production classes even if the intended business-policy change is Replay-only. | Replace the claim ƒ?oProduction is read-only/no rollbackƒ?? with a per-item impact statement. Any shared extraction or evidence capture must have Production regression tests, transaction/latency analysis and a code rollback path. No intended production trading-policy change is permitted. |
  20: | CR-03 | Current Replay misses production orchestration, not only wallet state. | Register the close-message/price-event lineage, label-free profit-lock exit, advisory stop-loss, SELL/add suppression during an active profit lock, progressive-add gate, analysis/fill clocks, historical-only/skipped candles, same-boundary higher-timeframe visibility and daily-budget timing. Each must be either implemented for parity, classified as an allowed divergence, or cause a parity refusal. |
  21: | CR-04 | ƒ?oParityƒ?? has no usable oracle after the virtual account diverges from Production. | Define separate outcomes: (a) **decision parity**, compared with stored as-of Production decision evidence; (b) **lifecycle parity**, only for an authorised matching virtual starting state; and (c) **simulation**, useful output that must never claim parity. Classify scheduling/concurrency differences explicitly rather than silently accepting them. |
  22: | CR-05 | Evidence qualification is incomplete and incorrectly treats deliberate exclusions as failures. | Define an era boundary and eligible evidence set. A parity run must use the same accepted/applied price observations Production could use; `NON_CANONICAL` and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` remain deliberate exclusions, not missing evidence. Require as-of candle identity/provenance, signal mode/outcome, configuration/version, sentiment availability time and applicable order-book/derivative evidence. A gap definition belongs in simulation diagnostics; parity refuses only when required accepted evidence is absent. |
  23: | CR-06 | Default `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` is not currently reachable for evidence eras with derivatives/configuration history missing. | Do not make an impossible mode the default. The replacement must either (1) register forward capture of the missing evidence and make parity available only from that capture date, or (2) require the caller to choose `SIMULATION` explicitly and reject a parity request with exact reasons. |
  24: | CR-07 | The base revision, migration sequencing and deployment facts conflict. | Freeze an exact Git commit/diff identity before design approval. Reconcile the observed migration/code state with AGENTS.md and actual Flyway history using read-only evidence. The replacement spec must state the base commit, migration numbers reserved, merge order, target database compatibility and the outcome if an expected table/migration is absent. |
  25: | CR-08 | Multi-symbol/global-budget ordering is not designed. | Either restrict the first parity scope to one virtual account/symbol with an explicit refusal for multi-symbol runs, or register deterministic cross-symbol scheduling and global-limit claim ordering in `ExecutionReplayScope` with tests. |
  26: | CR-09 | Replay-only removals are product decisions, not automatic parity repairs. | Do not remove FIX-11K, FIX-11Q, the pre-FIX122 selector, or `EXPERIMENTAL` mode in the parity change unless their callers, persisted data and user-facing workflows are inventoried and the user approves their retirement. Until then, isolate them from baseline parity and make their outcome clearly experimental. |
  27: 
  28: ## 3. Additional source findings to preserve in the next scope
  29: 
  30: The independent review identified source-backed gaps that must become registry
  31: items rather than prose aspirations:
  32: 
  33: - Replay currently can analyse a `closed=0` candle and use an earlier candle
  34:   snapshot. A parity run needs durable candle identity/finality checks.
  35: - The live close path and Replay do not necessarily see the same 1m decision
  36:   at a candle boundary. Event lineage, not a loose timestamp sort, must define
  37:   the replay ordering rule.
  38: - Production can subsequently close an active profit lock without rechecking
  39:   labels, while current Replay may retain the position. It also has an advisory
  40:   signal-path stop-loss that Replay does not invoke.
  41: - Historical derivatives/order-book/sentiment evidence may be unavailable or
  42:   have only event time rather than record/availability time. Unknown evidence
  43:   must produce a diagnostic refusal for parity, not a no-look-ahead claim.
  44: - A Replay account must never read Production `wallet_*` state. The forbidden
  45:   scope must name the Production tables precisely while allowing Replay-owned
  46:   test/result tables where approved.
  47: - A manual replay run executes in the same JVM and database pool as the trader.
  48:   Its resource limits and cancellation behaviour must protect Production
  49:   freshness and must be tested.
  50: 
  51: ## 4. Non-material corrections to include
  52: 
  53: - Include the current configuration values that affect sizing and daily limits,
  54:   including `max-open-positions` and `max-daily-loss-percent`.
  55: - Define behaviour when the Production daily-budget check would fail or throw.
  56: - Clear `RegimeStateService` replay state deterministically after every run.
  57: - State whether any new Replay evidence pre-write extends or replaces the
  58:   current pending-evidence guard.
  59: - Document that current paper trades use zero fees, so parity is not a claim
  60:   about exchange-net profitability.
  61: - Test cancellation/worker completion, two-request admission behaviour,
  62:   curated and random/incident windows, look-ahead, and refusal-rate reporting.
  63: 
  64: ## 5. Required next review package
  65: 
  66: Before another document gate, provide one exact revised specification and no
  67: implementation. It must include:
  68: 
  69: 1. A frozen source baseline and migration/deployment reconciliation.
  70: 2. The full artifact registry described in CR-01.
  71: 3. The three parity levels and their explicit oracles/refusal conditions.
  72: 4. The evidence-era and availability-time model.
  73: 5. The single-symbol-first or deterministic multi-symbol decision.
  74: 6. A user decision table for each proposed Replay-only retirement.
  75: 7. A complete test/validation/rollback/documentation plan.
  76: 
  77: Until then, `RPR-0.4-DRAFT` remains **REJECTED** and no source, database,
  78: configuration or deployment change is authorised.

 562:     private ExitDecision evaluateExit(long runId, ShadowPosition p, TradeSignal s, TradeSignal oneMinute, TradeSignal five, TradeSignal one) {
 563:         BigDecimal price = s.getLatestPrice();
 564:         if (price == null) return ExitDecision.hold();
 565: 
 566:         // Replay uses the exact same temporal price-authority rule as production. A signal
 567:         // generated later may still carry a candle close from before entry; that historical
 568:         // price may update MTF context but may not retroactively hit TP/SL/profit-lock.
 569:         boolean authoritativePrice = priceAuthorityPolicy.canUseSignalPrice(s, p.entryTime());
 570:         if (authoritativePrice && p.takeProfit() != null && price.compareTo(p.takeProfit()) >= 0) {
 571:             // Exact production TP-continuation call, including immutable entry structure.
 572:             // FIX-011 regression relies on Replay and live using this same shared policy.
 573:             PositionContinuationPolicy.Evaluation continuation = continuationPolicy.evaluate(
 574:                     oneMinute != null ? oneMinute : s, five, one,
 575:                     p.entryTrend(), p.entryStructure(), p.entryMomentum(), p.entryVolume(),
 576:                     p.entryConfidence(), p.entryScore());
 577:             if (continuation.extendTarget()) {
 578:                 BigDecimal distance = p.takeProfit().subtract(p.entryPrice());
 579:                 BigDecimal newTarget = p.takeProfit().add(distance.multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(0.50), MC), MC);
 580:                 persistManagement(runId, s, "TAKE_PROFIT_EXTENDED", p.takeProfit(), newTarget, p, continuation.explanation());
 581:                 jdbcTemplate.update("UPDATE wallet_position_test SET take_profit_usdt=? WHERE id=?", newTarget, p.positionId());
 582:                 return new ExitDecision(false, "EXTEND_TAKE_PROFIT", continuation.explanation(), newTarget);
 583:             }
 584:             oneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver.observeBaselineTakeProfitFailure(
 585:                     runId, s.getSymbol(), p.positionId(), s.getGeneratedAt(), price,
 586:                     p.entryTime(), p.entryPrice(), p.quantity(), p.cost(), p.stopLoss(), p.takeProfit(), p.highest(),
 587:                     p.profitLockActive(), p.profitLockPrice(), p.entryScore(), p.entryConfidence(), p.entryTrend(),
 588:                     p.entryStructure(), p.entryMomentum(), p.entryVolume(), oneMinute != null ? oneMinute : s, five, one,
 589:                     continuation.explanation());
 590:             persistManagement(runId, s, "TAKE_PROFIT_EXIT", p.takeProfit(), p.takeProfit(), p, continuation.explanation());
 591:             return new ExitDecision(true, "TAKE_PROFIT", continuation.explanation());
 592:         }
 593:         // FIX-052 fallback ordering also mirrors Production: STOP_LOSS is absolute and
 594:         // is evaluated before profit-lock. Exact V64 runs normally take this path from
 595:         // persisted live-price events rather than from signal-carried prices.
 596:         if (authoritativePrice && p.stopLoss() != null && price.compareTo(p.stopLoss()) <= 0)
 597:             return new ExitDecision(true, "STOP_LOSS", "Price reached the stored stop loss.");
 598: 
 599:         ShadowPosition updated = authoritativePrice ? profitLockState(p, price) : p;
 600:         if (authoritativePrice && (updated.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.ACTIVE
 601:                 || updated.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE
 602:                 || p.profitLockActive() != updated.profitLockActive()
 603:                 || !equalsNullable(p.profitLockPrice(), updated.profitLockPrice())
 604:                 || p.profitLockState() != updated.profitLockState())) {
 605:             BigDecimal fix118Progress = updated.takeProfit() == null || updated.takeProfit().compareTo(updated.entryPrice()) <= 0
 606:                     ? BigDecimal.ZERO
 607:                     : updated.highest().subtract(updated.entryPrice(), MC)
 608:                             .multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(100), MC)
 609:                             .divide(updated.takeProfit().subtract(updated.entryPrice(), MC), 6, RoundingMode.HALF_UP);
 610:             log.info("[FIX-118][REPLAY][SIGNAL_PATH] runId={}, positionId={}, signalId={}, symbol={}, generatedAt={}, price={}, " +
 611:                             "entry={}, target={}, highest={}, previousActive={}, active={}, previousState={}, state={}, previousLock={}, lock={}, progressPct={}, triggered={}",
 612:                     runId, updated.positionId(), s.getId(), s.getSymbol(), s.getGeneratedAt(), price,
 613:                     updated.entryPrice(), updated.takeProfit(), updated.highest(), p.profitLockActive(), updated.profitLockActive(),
 614:                     p.profitLockState(), updated.profitLockState(), p.profitLockPrice(), updated.profitLockPrice(), fix118Progress,
 615:                     updated.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.ACTIVE && updated.profitLockPrice() != null
 616:                             && price.compareTo(updated.profitLockPrice()) <= 0);
 617:         }
 618:         BigDecimal minimumProfitableExit = p.entryPrice().multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(1.0005));
 619:         if (authoritativePrice && updated.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE
 620:                 && price.compareTo(minimumProfitableExit) < 0) {
 621:             String floorReason = "TP-extension rebase safety floor was breached at " + minimumProfitableExit + ". "
 622:                     + profitLockConfigText();
 623:             log.info("[FIX-118][REPLAY][SIGNAL_REBASE_HARD_FLOOR] runId={}, positionId={}, signalId={}, symbol={}, generatedAt={}, price={}, floor={}",
 624:                     runId, updated.positionId(), s.getId(), s.getSymbol(), s.getGeneratedAt(), price, minimumProfitableExit);
 625:             persistManagement(runId, s, "PROFIT_LOCK_HARD_EXIT", p.takeProfit(), p.takeProfit(), updated, floorReason);
 626:             return new ExitDecision(true, "PROFIT_LOCK_HARD_EXIT", floorReason);
 627:         }
 628:         if (authoritativePrice && updated.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.ACTIVE && updated.profitLockPrice() != null
 629:                 && price.compareTo(updated.profitLockPrice()) <= 0) {
 630:             log.info("[FIX-118][REPLAY][SIGNAL_TRIGGER] runId={}, positionId={}, signalId={}, symbol={}, generatedAt={}, price={}, lock={}, target={}",
 631:                     runId, updated.positionId(), s.getId(), s.getSymbol(), s.getGeneratedAt(), price,
 632:                     updated.profitLockPrice(), updated.takeProfit());
 633:             if (price.compareTo(minimumProfitableExit) < 0) {
 634:                 String floorReason = "Profit-lock hard floor was breached; protected profit can no longer be preserved. "
 635:                         + profitLockConfigText();
 636:                 persistManagement(runId, s, "PROFIT_LOCK_HARD_EXIT", p.takeProfit(), p.takeProfit(), updated, floorReason);
 637:                 return new ExitDecision(true, "PROFIT_LOCK_HARD_EXIT", floorReason);
 638:             }
 639:             PositionExitPolicy.Evaluation lockDecision = exitPolicy.evaluateProfitLockBreach(
 640:                     oneMinute != null ? oneMinute : s, five, one);
 641:             if (!lockDecision.exit()) {
 642:                 String holdReason = lockDecision.explanation() + " " + profitLockConfigText();
 643:                 persistManagement(runId, s, "PROFIT_LOCK_HOLD", p.takeProfit(), p.takeProfit(), updated, holdReason);
 644:                 return ExitDecision.hold();
 645:             }
 646:             String lockReason = "Price retraced to the protected profit level after a profitable advance. "
 647:                     + lockDecision.explanation() + " " + profitLockConfigText();
 648:             persistManagement(runId, s, "PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT", p.takeProfit(), p.takeProfit(), updated, lockReason);
 649:             return new ExitDecision(true, lockDecision.code(), lockReason);
 650:         }
 651:         // Production has two SELL authorities: the live HTF PositionExitPolicy and the
 652:         // 1m signal TradeExecutionValidationService. Replay executes both shared production
 653:         // policies in the same order instead of carrying a test-only SELL rule.
 654:         PositionExitPolicy.Evaluation normalExit = exitPolicy.evaluateNormalExit(
 655:                 oneMinute != null ? oneMinute : s, five, one);
 656:         if (normalExit.exit()) {
 657:             return new ExitDecision(true, normalExit.code(), normalExit.explanation());
 658:         }
 659:         if ("1m".equals(s.getInterval()) && bearish(s.getDecision())) {
 660:             TradeExecutionValidationService.ValidationResult validatedSell = executionValidationService.validateSell(s);
 661:             if (validatedSell.allowed()) {
 662:                 return new ExitDecision(true, validatedSell.code(), validatedSell.explanation());
 663:             }
 664:         }
 665:         return ExitDecision.hold();
 666:     }

 668:     private FallbackNearTpEvaluation evaluateNearTpAtSignalFallback(long runId, ShadowPosition current,
 669:                                                                     TradeSignal signal, TradeSignal oneMinute,
 670:                                                                     TradeSignal fiveMinute) {
 671:         NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.State state = new NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.State(
 672:                 current.nearTpState(), current.nearTpBestPrice(), current.nearTpBearishStreak(),
 673:                 current.nearTpLastOneMinuteSignalId(), current.nearTpHarvestUsed());
 674:         NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.Evaluation evaluation = nearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.evaluate(
 675:                 state, current.entryPrice(), current.takeProfit(), signal.getLatestPrice(), signal.getGeneratedAt(),
 676:                 oneMinute, fiveMinute);
 677:         ShadowPosition updated = current.withNearTp(evaluation.state());
 678:         if (!updated.sameNearTp(current)) persistNearTpState(updated);
 679:         if (evaluation.transition()) {
 680:             log.info("FIX-11T Replay fallback Near-TP: runId={}, positionId={}, symbol={}, code={}, state={}, price={}, givebackPct={}, bearish1mStreak={}, detail={}",
 681:                     runId, updated.positionId(), signal.getSymbol(), evaluation.code(), updated.nearTpState(),
 682:                     signal.getLatestPrice(), evaluation.givebackPercent(), updated.nearTpBearishStreak(), evaluation.explanation());
 683:         }
 684:         if (!evaluation.harvestEligible()) return new FallbackNearTpEvaluation(updated, BigDecimal.ZERO, BigDecimal.ZERO);
 685: 
 686:         BigDecimal soldQty = updated.quantity().multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(0.50), MC);
 687:         BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy.Evaluation minimumCheck =
 688:                 binanceMinimumExecutionPolicy.evaluate(signal.getSymbol(), soldQty, signal.getLatestPrice());
 689:         if (!minimumCheck.executable()) {
 690:             updated = updated.withNearTp(new NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.State(
 691:                     NearTpState.NEAR_TP_REJECTION_DETECTED, updated.nearTpBestPrice(),
 692:                     0, updated.nearTpLastOneMinuteSignalId(), false));
 693:             persistNearTpState(updated);
 694:             log.info("FIX-11T Replay fallback NEAR_TP_HARVEST_SKIPPED: runId={}, positionId={}, symbol={}, reason={}, requestedQty={}, requestedNotional={}, binanceMinimum={}; existing position management continues",
 695:                     runId, updated.positionId(), signal.getSymbol(), minimumCheck.code(), soldQty,
 696:                     minimumCheck.requestedNotional(), minimumCheck.minimumNotional());

 109:      * FIX-112C historical replay path. Replay reads persisted Production Order Book
 110:      * observations strictly as-of the historical evaluation timestamp and routes them

 112:      * with no persisted evidence remain explicitly UNAVAILABLE.

 116:             String interval,

 118:             boolean entryAllowed,
 119:             BigDecimal currentPrice,

 127:             return result(LiquidityContextStatus.DISABLED, currentDecision, currentDecision,

 134:         // whose observed_at is inside the exact as-of window. No live Binance book
 135:         // and no future observation can enter a historical decision.

 144:                         // silently change that established Production behavior during Replay.

 146:                                 || row.bestBid().add(row.bestAsk()).signum() <= 0

 152:                                         row.askWallPrice(), row.askWallQuantity())))
 153:                 .toList();

 155:             return result(LiquidityContextStatus.UNAVAILABLE, currentDecision, currentDecision,
 156:                     entryAllowed, null, null, null, null, null, null, null, null,
 157:                     false, false, 0,
 158:                     "No persisted Production order-book observation exists for this historical replay window; live data was not substituted.",
 159:                     snapshotTime, policy, 0L);
 160:         }
 161:         return evaluateNormalized(symbol, interval, currentDecision, entryAllowed,
 162:                 currentPrice, stopLoss, takeProfit, snapshotTime, policy, observations);

   1: package com.crypto.regression.service;
   2: 
   3: import com.crypto.domain.TradeSignal;
   4: import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
   5: 
   6: import java.util.ArrayList;
   7: import java.util.List;
   8: import java.util.Objects;
   9: 
  10: /**
  11:  * FIX-091 / Fix 6: field-level pre-wallet parity comparator.
  12:  * It deliberately compares trading-intelligence state only; wallet execution is outside scope.
  13:  */
  14: @Service
  15: public class ReplayParityValidator {
  16: 
  17:     public Result compare(TradeSignal production, TradeSignal replay) {
  18:         List<String> mismatches = new ArrayList<>();
  19:         if (production == null || replay == null) {
  20:             mismatches.add("Production and Replay signals must both exist");
  21:             return new Result(false, mismatches);
  22:         }
  23: 
  24:         same(mismatches, "detectedRegime", production.getDetectedRegime(), replay.getDetectedRegime());
  25:         same(mismatches, "candidateRegime", production.getCandidateRegime(), replay.getCandidateRegime());
  26:         same(mismatches, "confirmedRegime", production.getConfirmedRegime(), replay.getConfirmedRegime());
  27:         same(mismatches, "selectedStrategy", production.getSelectedStrategy(), replay.getSelectedStrategy());
  28:         same(mismatches, "originalDecision", production.getOriginalDecision(), replay.getOriginalDecision());
  29:         same(mismatches, "finalDecision", production.getDecision(), replay.getDecision());
  30:         same(mismatches, "rawConfidence", production.getRawConfidenceScore(), replay.getRawConfidenceScore());
  31:         same(mismatches, "effectiveConfidence", production.getEffectiveConfidenceScore(), replay.getEffectiveConfidenceScore());
  32:         same(mismatches, "primaryBlockingStage", production.getPrimaryBlockingStage(), replay.getPrimaryBlockingStage());
  33:         same(mismatches, "btcContextStatus", production.getBtcContextStatus(), replay.getBtcContextStatus());
  34:         same(mismatches, "liquidityStatus", production.getLiquidityStatus(), replay.getLiquidityStatus());
  35:         same(mismatches, "entryAuthority", production.getEntryAuthority(), replay.getEntryAuthority());
  36:         same(mismatches, "entryAuthorityMaxPositionPercent",
  37:                 production.getEntryAuthorityMaxPositionPercent(), replay.getEntryAuthorityMaxPositionPercent());
  38:         return new Result(mismatches.isEmpty(), List.copyOf(mismatches));
  39:     }
  40: 
  41:     private void same(List<String> mismatches, String field, Object expected, Object actual) {
  42:         if (!Objects.equals(expected, actual)) {
  43:             mismatches.add(field + ": Production=" + expected + ", Replay=" + actual);
  44:         }
  45:     }
  46: 
  47:     public record Result(boolean matches, List<String> mismatches) {}
  48: }

 562:     private ExitDecision evaluateExit(long runId, ShadowPosition p, TradeSignal s, TradeSignal oneMinute, TradeSignal five, TradeSignal one) {
 563:         BigDecimal price = s.getLatestPrice();
 564:         if (price == null) return ExitDecision.hold();
 565: 
 566:         // Replay uses the exact same temporal price-authority rule as production. A signal
 567:         // generated later may still carry a candle close from before entry; that historical
 568:         // price may update MTF context but may not retroactively hit TP/SL/profit-lock.
 569:         boolean authoritativePrice = priceAuthorityPolicy.canUseSignalPrice(s, p.entryTime());
 570:         if (authoritativePrice && p.takeProfit() != null && price.compareTo(p.takeProfit()) >= 0) {
 571:             // Exact production TP-continuation call, including immutable entry structure.
 572:             // FIX-011 regression relies on Replay and live using this same shared policy.
 573:             PositionContinuationPolicy.Evaluation continuation = continuationPolicy.evaluate(
 574:                     oneMinute != null ? oneMinute : s, five, one,
 575:                     p.entryTrend(), p.entryStructure(), p.entryMomentum(), p.entryVolume(),
 576:                     p.entryConfidence(), p.entryScore());
 577:             if (continuation.extendTarget()) {
 578:                 BigDecimal distance = p.takeProfit().subtract(p.entryPrice());
 579:                 BigDecimal newTarget = p.takeProfit().add(distance.multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(0.50), MC), MC);
 580:                 persistManagement(runId, s, "TAKE_PROFIT_EXTENDED", p.takeProfit(), newTarget, p, continuation.explanation());
 581:                 jdbcTemplate.update("UPDATE wallet_position_test SET take_profit_usdt=? WHERE id=?", newTarget, p.positionId());
 582:                 return new ExitDecision(false, "EXTEND_TAKE_PROFIT", continuation.explanation(), newTarget);
 583:             }
 584:             oneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver.observeBaselineTakeProfitFailure(
 585:                     runId, s.getSymbol(), p.positionId(), s.getGeneratedAt(), price,
 586:                     p.entryTime(), p.entryPrice(), p.quantity(), p.cost(), p.stopLoss(), p.takeProfit(), p.highest(),
 587:                     p.profitLockActive(), p.profitLockPrice(), p.entryScore(), p.entryConfidence(), p.entryTrend(),
 588:                     p.entryStructure(), p.entryMomentum(), p.entryVolume(), oneMinute != null ? oneMinute : s, five, one,
 589:                     continuation.explanation());
 590:             persistManagement(runId, s, "TAKE_PROFIT_EXIT", p.takeProfit(), p.takeProfit(), p, continuation.explanation());
 591:             return new ExitDecision(true, "TAKE_PROFIT", continuation.explanation());
 592:         }
 593:         // FIX-052 fallback ordering also mirrors Production: STOP_LOSS is absolute and
 594:         // is evaluated before profit-lock. Exact V64 runs normally take this path from
 595:         // persisted live-price events rather than from signal-carried prices.
 596:         if (authoritativePrice && p.stopLoss() != null && price.compareTo(p.stopLoss()) <= 0)
 597:             return new ExitDecision(true, "STOP_LOSS", "Price reached the stored stop loss.");
 598: 
 599:         ShadowPosition updated = authoritativePrice ? profitLockState(p, price) : p;
 600:         if (authoritativePrice && (updated.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.ACTIVE
 601:                 || updated.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE
 602:                 || p.profitLockActive() != updated.profitLockActive()
 603:                 || !equalsNullable(p.profitLockPrice(), updated.profitLockPrice())
 604:                 || p.profitLockState() != updated.profitLockState())) {

 606:                     ? BigDecimal.ZERO

 608:                             .multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(100), MC)

 610:             log.info("[FIX-118][REPLAY][SIGNAL_PATH] runId={}, positionId={}, signalId={}, symbol={}, generatedAt={}, price={}, " +

 615:                     updated.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.ACTIVE && updated.profitLockPrice() != null

 617:         }
 618:         BigDecimal minimumProfitableExit = p.entryPrice().multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(1.0005));
 619:         if (authoritativePrice && updated.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE
 620:                 && price.compareTo(minimumProfitableExit) < 0) {

 622:                     + profitLockConfigText();

 627:         }
 628:         if (authoritativePrice && updated.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.ACTIVE && updated.profitLockPrice() != null

 650:         }
 651:         // Production has two SELL authorities: the live HTF PositionExitPolicy and the

 653:         // policies in the same order instead of carrying a test-only SELL rule.

 656:         if (normalExit.exit()) {
 657:             return new ExitDecision(true, normalExit.code(), normalExit.explanation());
 658:         }
 659:         if ("1m".equals(s.getInterval()) && bearish(s.getDecision())) {
 660:             TradeExecutionValidationService.ValidationResult validatedSell = executionValidationService.validateSell(s);
 661:             if (validatedSell.allowed()) {
 662:                 return new ExitDecision(true, validatedSell.code(), validatedSell.explanation());
 663:             }
 664:         }
 665:         return ExitDecision.hold();
 666:     }

 668:     private FallbackNearTpEvaluation evaluateNearTpAtSignalFallback(long runId, ShadowPosition current,
 669:                                                                     TradeSignal signal, TradeSignal oneMinute,
 670:                                                                     TradeSignal fiveMinute) {
 671:         NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.State state = new NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.State(
 672:                 current.nearTpState(), current.nearTpBestPrice(), current.nearTpBearishStreak(),
 673:                 current.nearTpLastOneMinuteSignalId(), current.nearTpHarvestUsed());

 675:                 state, current.entryPrice(), current.takeProfit(), signal.getLatestPrice(), signal.getGeneratedAt(),
 676:                 oneMinute, fiveMinute);

 678:         if (!updated.sameNearTp(current)) persistNearTpState(updated);
 679:         if (evaluation.transition()) {
 680:             log.info("FIX-11T Replay fallback Near-TP: runId={}, positionId={}, symbol={}, code={}, state={}, price={}, givebackPct={}, bearish1mStreak={}, detail={}",
 681:                     runId, updated.positionId(), signal.getSymbol(), evaluation.code(), updated.nearTpState(),

 683:         }

 696:                     minimumCheck.requestedNotional(), minimumCheck.minimumNotional());
 697:             return new FallbackNearTpEvaluation(updated, BigDecimal.ZERO, BigDecimal.ZERO);
 698:         }
 699:         BigDecimal proceeds = soldQty.multiply(signal.getLatestPrice(), MC);
 700:         BigDecimal soldCost = updated.entryPrice().multiply(soldQty, MC);
 701:         BigDecimal pnl = proceeds.subtract(soldCost, MC);
 702:         BigDecimal pnlPct = soldCost.signum() == 0 ? BigDecimal.ZERO : pnl.divide(soldCost, 8, RoundingMode.HALF_UP)
 703:                 .multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(100));

 707:                 runId, updated.positionId(), signal.getSymbol(), soldQty, updated.quantity(), signal.getLatestPrice(), pnl);
 708:         return new FallbackNearTpEvaluation(updated, proceeds, pnl);
 709:     }

 663:             }

 668:     private FallbackNearTpEvaluation evaluateNearTpAtSignalFallback(long runId, ShadowPosition current,
 669:                                                                     TradeSignal signal, TradeSignal oneMinute,
 670:                                                                     TradeSignal fiveMinute) {

 703:                 .multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(100));

  20:  * This class deliberately checks only the agreed minimum executable notional boundary.
  21:  * It does not add a new Near-TP-specific step-size/precision model.
  22:  */
  23: @Component
  24: @RequiredArgsConstructor
  25: @Slf4j

  27:     private static final MathContext MC = MathContext.DECIMAL64;
  28: 
  29:     private final BinanceMarketDataClient binanceMarketDataClient;
  30:     public Evaluation evaluate(String symbol, BigDecimal quantity, BigDecimal price) {
  31:         if (symbol == null || symbol.isBlank() || quantity == null || quantity.signum() <= 0
  32:                 || price == null || price.signum() <= 0) {
  33:             return new Evaluation(false, "INVALID_ORDER_INPUT", BigDecimal.ZERO, null);
  34:         }
  35:         String pair = symbol.trim().toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT);
  36:         BigDecimal notional = quantity.multiply(price, MC);
  37:         BigDecimal minimum;
  38:         try {
  39:             minimum = binanceMarketDataClient.getMinimumExecutableNotional(pair);
  40:         } catch (RuntimeException ex) {
  41:             // Fail safe. Missing exchange constraints must never be converted into permission
  42:             // to create an order that real Binance might reject.
  43:             log.warn("FIX-11T Binance minimum executable amount unavailable: symbol={}, requestedNotional={}, error={}",
  44:                     pair, notional, ex.getMessage());
  45:             return new Evaluation(false, "BINANCE_MINIMUM_UNAVAILABLE", notional, null);
  46:         }
  47:         if (notional.compareTo(minimum) < 0) {
  48:             return new Evaluation(false, "BELOW_BINANCE_MINIMUM", notional, minimum);
  49:         }
  50:         return new Evaluation(true, "EXECUTABLE", notional, minimum);
  51:     }

  53:     public record Evaluation(boolean executable, String code, BigDecimal requestedNotional, BigDecimal minimumNotional) {
  54:     }
  55: }

 153:     /**
 154:      * FIX-11T: read the symbol's current Binance minimum executable notional directly
 155:      * from exchangeInfo.  Near-TP uses this only as a minimum-order guard; it does not
 156:      * introduce a separate quantity-rounding model. NOTIONAL is preferred when present,
 157:      * with MIN_NOTIONAL retained for symbols that still expose the older filter.
 158:      */
 159:     public BigDecimal getMinimumExecutableNotional(String symbol) {
 160:         if (symbol == null || symbol.isBlank()) {
 161:             throw new IllegalArgumentException("Symbol is required");
 162:         }
 163: 
 164:         Map<String, Object> response = request(() -> restClient.get()
 165:             .uri(uriBuilder -> uriBuilder
 166:                 .path("/api/v3/exchangeInfo")
 167:                 .queryParam("symbol", symbol.toUpperCase())
 168:                 .build())
 169:             .retrieve()
 170:             .body(new ParameterizedTypeReference<>() {
 171:             }));
 172: 
 173:         if (response == null) {
 174:             throw new IllegalStateException("Empty Binance exchangeInfo response for " + symbol);
 175:         }
 176:         Object symbolsValue = response.get("symbols");
 177:         if (!(symbolsValue instanceof List<?> symbols) || symbols.isEmpty() || !(symbols.get(0) instanceof Map<?, ?> symbolInfo)) {
 178:             throw new IllegalStateException("Binance exchangeInfo did not contain symbol rules for " + symbol);
 179:         }
 180:         Object filtersValue = symbolInfo.get("filters");
 181:         if (!(filtersValue instanceof List<?> filters)) {
 182:             throw new IllegalStateException("Binance exchangeInfo did not contain filters for " + symbol);
 183:         }
 184: 
 185:         BigDecimal minNotional = null;
 186:         for (Object filterValue : filters) {
 187:             if (!(filterValue instanceof Map<?, ?> filter)) continue;
 188:             String filterType = String.valueOf(filter.get("filterType"));
 189:             if (!"NOTIONAL".equals(filterType) && !"MIN_NOTIONAL".equals(filterType)) continue;
 190:             Object raw = filter.get("minNotional");
 191:             if (raw == null) continue;
 192:             BigDecimal candidate = new BigDecimal(String.valueOf(raw));
 193:             if (candidate.signum() <= 0) continue;
 194:             if ("NOTIONAL".equals(filterType)) return candidate;
 195:             minNotional = candidate;
 196:         }
 197:         if (minNotional != null) return minNotional;
 198:         throw new IllegalStateException("Binance exchangeInfo did not expose a minimum notional for " + symbol);
 199:     }

  40:      * FIX-112C parity-safety boundary: enqueue persistence and return immediately to the

  61:             });

  52:     public DerivativesPositioningResult evaluate(String symbol, String interval, SignalDecision decision,
  53:                                                   boolean entryAllowed, Instant evaluatedAt) {
  54:         Instant now = evaluatedAt == null ? Instant.now() : evaluatedAt;
  55:         if (!properties.enabled()) return result(DerivativesPositioningStatus.NOT_APPLICABLE, decision, entryAllowed,
  56:                 null, null, null, null, null, null, 0, period(interval), 0,
  57:                 "Derivatives positioning is disabled.", now);
  58:         try {
  59:             List<BinanceFundingRate> funding = client.fundingHistory(symbol, properties.fundingHistoryLimit());
  60:             List<BinanceOpenInterestPoint> oi = client.openInterestHistory(symbol, period(interval), properties.openInterestHistoryLimit());
  61:             if (funding.isEmpty() || oi.size() < 2) {
  62:                 return result(DerivativesPositioningStatus.LEARNING, decision, entryAllowed,
  63:                         latestFunding(funding), percentile(funding), latestOi(oi), latestOiValue(oi), null,
  64:                         priceChange(symbol, interval), funding.size(), period(interval), -5,
  65:                         "Funding or open-interest history is still insufficient; no veto was applied.", now);
  66:             }
  67:             BigDecimal fundingRate = latestFunding(funding);
  68:             BigDecimal fundingPercentile = percentile(funding);
  69:             BigDecimal oiChange = percentChange(oi.get(oi.size() - 2).sumOpenInterest(), oi.get(oi.size() - 1).sumOpenInterest());
  70:             BigDecimal priceChange = priceChange(symbol, interval);
  71:             DerivativesPositioningStatus status = classify(fundingRate, oiChange, priceChange);
  72:             SignalDecision finalDecision = decision;
  73:             boolean allowed = entryAllowed;
  74:             int confidence = confidence(status);
  75:             if (isBullish(decision) && status == DerivativesPositioningStatus.LONGS_CROWDED
  76:                     && properties.vetoExtremeCrowding()
  77:                     && oiChange != null && oiChange.abs().compareTo(properties.strongOpenInterestChangeThreshold()) >= 0) {
  78:                 finalDecision = SignalDecision.WATCH;
  79:                 allowed = false;
  80:             }
  81:             if (isBearish(decision) && status == DerivativesPositioningStatus.SHORTS_CROWDED
  82:                     && properties.vetoExtremeCrowding()
  83:                     && oiChange != null && oiChange.abs().compareTo(properties.strongOpenInterestChangeThreshold()) >= 0) {
  84:                 finalDecision = SignalDecision.NEUTRAL;
  85:                 allowed = false;
  86:             }
  87:             String explanation = explanation(status, fundingRate, fundingPercentile, oiChange, priceChange, allowed);
  88:             BinanceOpenInterestPoint latest = oi.get(oi.size() - 1);
  89:             return result(status, finalDecision, allowed, fundingRate, fundingPercentile,
  90:                     latest.sumOpenInterest(), latest.sumOpenInterestValue(), oiChange, priceChange,
  91:                     funding.size(), period(interval), confidence, explanation, now);
  92:         } catch (RestClientResponseException ex) {
  93:             if (ex.getStatusCode().value() == 400) {
  94:                 return result(DerivativesPositioningStatus.NOT_APPLICABLE, decision, entryAllowed,
  95:                         null, null, null, null, null, null, 0, period(interval), 0,
  96:                         "No matching Binance USD-M perpetual contract is available for " + symbol + ".", now);
  97:             }
  98:             return result(DerivativesPositioningStatus.UNAVAILABLE, decision, entryAllowed,
  99:                     null, null, null, null, null, null, 0, period(interval), -10,
 100:                     "Binance futures positioning data is unavailable: " + ex.getMessage(), now);
 101:         } catch (RuntimeException ex) {
 102:             return result(DerivativesPositioningStatus.UNAVAILABLE, decision, entryAllowed,
 103:                     null, null, null, null, null, null, 0, period(interval), -10,
 104:                     "Binance futures positioning data is unavailable: " + ex.getMessage(), now);
 105:         }
 106:     }
 107: 
 108:     private DerivativesPositioningStatus classify(BigDecimal funding, BigDecimal oiChange, BigDecimal priceChange) {
 109:         if (funding != null && funding.compareTo(properties.extremeFundingRate()) >= 0) return DerivativesPositioningStatus.LONGS_CROWDED;
 110:         if (funding != null && funding.compareTo(properties.extremeFundingRate().negate()) <= 0) return DerivativesPositioningStatus.SHORTS_CROWDED;
 111:         if (oiChange == null || priceChange == null) return DerivativesPositioningStatus.BALANCED;
 112:         boolean oiRising = oiChange.compareTo(properties.openInterestChangeThreshold()) >= 0;
 113:         boolean oiFalling = oiChange.compareTo(properties.openInterestChangeThreshold().negate()) <= 0;
 114:         if (priceChange.signum() > 0 && oiRising) return DerivativesPositioningStatus.FRESH_LONG_BUILDUP;
 115:         if (priceChange.signum() < 0 && oiRising) return DerivativesPositioningStatus.FRESH_SHORT_BUILDUP;
 116:         if (priceChange.signum() > 0 && oiFalling) return DerivativesPositioningStatus.SHORT_COVERING;
 117:         if (priceChange.signum() < 0 && oiFalling) return DerivativesPositioningStatus.LONG_LIQUIDATION;
 118:         if (funding != null && funding.compareTo(properties.moderateFundingRate()) >= 0) return DerivativesPositioningStatus.HEALTHY_BULLISH;
 119:         if (funding != null && funding.compareTo(properties.moderateFundingRate().negate()) <= 0) return DerivativesPositioningStatus.HEALTHY_BEARISH;
 120:         return oiChange.abs().compareTo(properties.openInterestChangeThreshold()) < 0
 121:                 ? DerivativesPositioningStatus.LOW_CONVICTION : DerivativesPositioningStatus.BALANCED;
 122:     }
 123: 
 124:     private int confidence(DerivativesPositioningStatus status) {
 125:         return switch (status) {
 126:             case FRESH_LONG_BUILDUP, FRESH_SHORT_BUILDUP -> 8;
 127:             case HEALTHY_BULLISH, HEALTHY_BEARISH, BALANCED -> 3;
 128:             case SHORT_COVERING, LONG_LIQUIDATION, LOW_CONVICTION -> -5;
 129:             case LONGS_CROWDED, SHORTS_CROWDED -> -15;
 130:             case LEARNING -> -5;
 131:             case UNAVAILABLE -> -10;
 132:             case NOT_APPLICABLE -> 0;
 133:         };
 134:     }
 135: 
 136:     private String explanation(DerivativesPositioningStatus status, BigDecimal funding, BigDecimal percentile,
 137:                                BigDecimal oiChange, BigDecimal priceChange, boolean allowed) {
 138:         return "Positioning " + status + "; funding=" + value(funding) + ", percentile=" + value(percentile)
 139:                 + "%, OI change=" + value(oiChange) + "%, price change=" + value(priceChange)
 140:                 + "%. Entry " + (allowed ? "remains allowed" : "was blocked") + ".";
 141:     }

 143:     private BigDecimal priceChange(String symbol, String interval) {
 144:         List<Candle> candles = new ArrayList<>(candleRepository.findClosedCandles(symbol, interval, PageRequest.of(0, 2)));
 145:         if (candles.size() < 2) return null;
 146:         candles.sort(Comparator.comparing(Candle::getOpenTime));
 147:         return percentChange(candles.get(0).getClosePrice(), candles.get(1).getClosePrice());
 148:     }

  30:     public StrategyProfile select(MarketRegimeAssessment assessment, MarketContextSnapshot context) {
  31:         if (!properties.isEnabled()) {
  32:             return profile(TradingStrategy.TREND_FOLLOWING, properties.getTrendFollowing(), true,
  33:                     "Dynamic strategy selection is disabled; using the default profile");
  34:         }
  35:         if (context != null && !context.dataValid()) {

  37:                     "Required market data is invalid or incomplete; new entries are disabled");
  38:         }
  39:         if (context != null && context.liquidityRisky()) {
  40:             return profile(TradingStrategy.DEFENSIVE, properties.getDefensive(), true,
  41:                     "Current order-book liquidity risk selected the defensive profile");
  42:         }
  43:         if (context != null && (context.derivativesStatus() == com.crypto.domain.DerivativesPositioningStatus.LONGS_CROWDED
  44:                 || context.derivativesStatus() == com.crypto.domain.DerivativesPositioningStatus.SHORTS_CROWDED)) {
  45:             return profile(TradingStrategy.DEFENSIVE, properties.getDefensive(), true,

  47:         }

  49:                 && context.sentimentCoverage().compareTo(java.math.BigDecimal.valueOf(0.50)) < 0) {

  52:         }

  54:             return profile(TradingStrategy.DEFENSIVE, properties.getDefensive(), true,
  55:                     "Strong higher-timeframe conflict selected the defensive profile");
  56:         }

 229:         return switch (status) {

 239:     private int btcConfidence(BtcContextStatus status, boolean stable) {
 240:         int base = switch (status) {
 241:             case CONFIRMED -> 95;
 242:             case NOT_APPLICABLE -> 85;
 243:             case NEUTRAL -> 75;
 244:             case LEARNING -> 50;
 245:             case CONFLICT -> 30;
 246:             case STRONG_CONFLICT -> 10;
 247:             case UNAVAILABLE -> 40;
 248:             case STALE_CONTEXT -> 0;
 249:         };
 250:         return stable ? base : Math.max(0, base - 10);
 251:     }
